{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf, so this yields an action of
{\mathbb{Z}}
on
{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}
. The definition of a
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf implies that
\deg{\mathscr{E}}_{i+l}=\deg{\mathscr{E}}_{i}+nl
, so the degree of
[l]({\mathcal{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
is
l
plus that of
({\mathcal{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
. This allows us to identify the quotient stack
{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}/{\mathbb{Z}}
with

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}/{\mathbb{Z}}={\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I,0}

as stacks over
\kappa
. The translation action of
{\mathbb{Z}}
commutes with restriction morphisms as well as the Hecke action, so we obtain a similar description of
{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{\mathscr{D}}^{\infty}/{\mathbb{Z}}
. The Hecke action also descends to
{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}/{\mathbb{Z}}
. By passing to this quotient stack (or equivalently, by restricting the degree), we obtain the following finite-type representability result.

Proposition

(
[
35
, (6.2)]
)
.

Suppose
I
is nonempty. Then
{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}/{\mathbb{Z}}
is actually a projective scheme over
C^{\prime}\smallsetminus(I\cup\infty)
.

Thus
{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{\mathscr{D}}^{\infty}/{\mathbb{Z}}=\varprojlim_{I}{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}/{\mathbb{Z}}
is also representable by a scheme.

5.14.

Before we proceed to the cohomology of our moduli spaces, we introduce a covering of
{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}
which we shall use to construct our coefficient sheaves. Write
\overline{B}
for the central division algebra over
\mathbf{F}_{\infty}
of Hasse invariant
-\frac{1}{n}
.

Proposition

(
[
35
, (8.11)]
)
.

There exists a pro-Galois covering

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell}_{{\mathscr{D}},I}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I},

where
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell}_{{\mathscr{D}},I}
is a scheme, whose Galois group is given by a right action of
\overline{B}^{\times}/\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}
. This covering is compatible with restriction morphisms
{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I^{\prime}}
, so taking the inverse limit yields an analogous pro-Galois covering

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell}_{\mathscr{D}}^{\infty}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{\mathscr{D}}^{\infty}

whose Galois group is given by a right action of
\overline{B}^{\times}/\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Briefly, the covering
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell}_{{\mathscr{D}},I}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}
is given as follows. Given a
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
, we can construct an object resembling a local shtuka at the place
\infty
(in a fashion similar to the construction to be given in
5.22
). This “local shtuka at
\infty
” will always be isomorphic to a fixed object analogous to the local shtuka of slope
-\frac{1}{n}
under the Dieudonné–Manin classification
[
34
, (2.4.5)]
, and the covering
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell}_{{\mathscr{D}},I}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}
parametrizes isomorphisms between our “local shtuka at
\infty
” and this fixed object. The Galois action is given by composition with this isomorphism, as the endomorphism ring of a local shtuka of slope
-\frac{1}{n}
at
\infty
is
\overline{B}
.

The stack
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell}_{{\mathscr{D}},I}
also has an action of
{\mathbb{Z}}
by translation, and it is preserved under the morphism
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell}_{{\mathscr{D}},I}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}
. Thus we obtain a pro-Galois morphism
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell}_{{\mathscr{D}},I}/{\mathbb{Z}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}/{\mathbb{Z}}
whose Galois group is given by a right action of
\overline{B}^{\times}/\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}
. This yields a similar pro-Galois morphism
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell}_{\mathscr{D}}^{\infty}/{\mathbb{Z}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{\mathscr{D}}^{\infty}/{\mathbb{Z}}
as well.

The space
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell}_{\mathscr{D}}^{\infty}
is the equi-characteristic analog of the Hermitian symmetric space covering a Shimura variety.

5.15.

At this point, we can finally introduce the cohomology of our moduli spaces. Fix a separable closure
\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}
of
\mathbf{F}
, and view all separable extensions of
\mathbf{F}
as lying in
\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}
. For every place
x
of
C
, choose a separable closure
\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}_{x}
of
\mathbf{F}_{x}
, fix an embedding
\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow}}\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}_{x}
, and form the absolute Galois groups
G_{\mathbf{F}}
and
G_{x}
of
\mathbf{F}
and
\mathbf{F}_{x}
, respectively, with respect to these separable closures. Write
{\mathbb{C}}_{x}
for the completion of
\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}_{x}
. We view
G_{x}
as a subgroup of
G_{\mathbf{F}}
via our embedding, and we denote the arithmetic
q_{x}
-Frobenius at
x
using
\sigma_{x}
.

Fix an irreducible smooth representation

\displaystyle\xi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\overline{B}^{\times}/\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\GL_{N}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}).

Since
\overline{B}^{\times}/\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}
is compact, we see
\xi
has finite image and is therefore defined over a finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\ell}
in
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
. Write
{\mathcal{L}}_{\xi,I}
for the
\ell
-adic sheaf on
{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}/{\mathbb{Z}}
induced from
\xi
and the pro-Galois morphism
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell}_{{\mathscr{D}},I}/{\mathbb{Z}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}/{\mathbb{Z}}
via monodromy, and write
{\mathcal{L}}_{\xi}
for the analogous
\ell
-adic sheaf on
{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell^{\infty}_{\mathscr{D}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
. Form the
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
-vector space

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\xi,\eta}\coloneqq H^{i}({\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell^{\infty}_{{\mathscr{D}},\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}}/{\mathbb{Z}},{\mathcal{L}}_{\xi})=\varinjlim_{I}H^{i}({\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I,\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}}/{\mathbb{Z}},{\mathcal{L}}_{\xi,I,\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}}),

where
I
runs over all finite closed subschemes of
C\smallsetminus\infty
. This has a left action of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}\times G_{\mathbf{F}}
, and it vanishes for sufficiently large
i
. Proposition
5.8
implies that

\displaystyle(H^{i}_{\xi,\eta})^{{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty}}=H^{i}({\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I,\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}}/{\mathbb{Z}},{\mathcal{L}}_{\xi,I,\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}}),

which shows that
H^{i}_{\xi,\eta}
is an admissible/continuous representation as in
[
20
, p. 24]
of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}\times G_{\mathbf{F}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
. Furthermore, for any
g
in
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}
, the action of

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\vol({\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty})}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty}g{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty}}\in C^{\infty}_{c}((D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times})

on
H^{i}_{\xi,\eta}
is induced by a correspondence
[
35
, (7.5)]

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},\mathbf{F}}^{\infty}/({\mathbb{Z}}\times({\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty}\cap g^{-1}{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty}g))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{1,\mathbf{F}}}
\scriptstyle{c_{2,\mathbf{F}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},\mathbf{F}}^{\infty}/({\mathbb{Z}}\times{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty})}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},\mathbf{F}}^{\infty}/({\mathbb{Z}}\times{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty})}

over
\mathbf{F}
, where
c_{1,\mathbf{F}}
is induced by further quotienting by
{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty}
, and
c_{2,\mathbf{F}}
is induced by the right action of
g^{-1}
followed by quotienting by
{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty}
. This is the equi-characteristic analog of
Hecke correspondences
.

Proposition
5.8
implies that
c_{1,\mathbf{F}}
and
c_{2,\mathbf{F}}
are finite étale. We write
c_{\mathbf{F}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I,\mathbf{F}}/{\mathbb{Z}}\dashrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I,\mathbf{F}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
for the correspondence formed by
c_{1,\mathbf{F}}
and
c_{2,\mathbf{F}}
, and we write
[H_{\xi}]
for the virtual representation
[H_{\xi}]\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{i}H^{i}_{\xi,\eta}
.

5.16.

Now we introduce a construction that takes any
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf over a certain base and yields an object resembling a local shtuka. Let
o
be a closed point in
C^{\prime}\smallsetminus\infty
, let
S
be a scheme over
\Spec{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
, and let
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
be a
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf over
S
.

Let
\Gamma_{o}
be the image of the graph of
i_{o}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}S\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}C\times_{\kappa}S
, and denote completions along
\Gamma_{o}
using
(-)^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{o}}
. Then
({\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{o}}
is a vector bundle on
(C\times_{\kappa}S)^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{o}}
of rank
n^{2}
with a right action of
{\mathscr{D}}_{o}=\M_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
, and
(j_{1})^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{o}}
is an isomorphism because
\Gamma_{o}
lies away from
\infty
. Thus we may form the composed morphism

\displaystyle(j_{1})^{\wedge,-1}_{\Gamma_{o}}\circ(t_{1})^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{o}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}({}^{\sigma}\!{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{o}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}({\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{o}}.

We see that the adic pullback of
(j_{1})^{\wedge,-1}_{\Gamma_{o}}\circ(t_{1})^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{o}}
as in
[
7
, p. 370]
to
\mathbf{F}_{o}
is an isomorphism, and its cokernel is a vector bundle on
S
of rank
n
.

We may identify
(C\times_{\kappa}S)^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{o}}
with the locally ringed space whose support is
{\lvert S\rvert}
and whose structure sheaf is
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}[\![\varpi_{o}]\!]

[
1
, Lemma 5.3]
. From this point of view, the pair

\displaystyle(({\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{o}},(j_{1})^{\wedge,-1}_{\Gamma_{o}}\circ(t_{1})^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{o}})

corresponds to a pair
({\mathscr{M}}_{o},{\mathscr{F}}_{o})
, where
{\mathscr{M}}_{o}
is a locally free
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}[\![\varpi_{o}]\!]
-module of rank
n^{2}
with a right
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}[\![\varpi_{o}]\!]
-linear action of
\M_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
, and
{\mathscr{F}}_{o}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\sigma^{*}_{o}{\mathscr{M}}_{o}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{M}}_{o}
is an
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}[\![\varpi_{o}]\!]
-linear morphism such that
\coker{\mathscr{F}}_{o}
is a locally free
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}
-module of rank
n
. Note that
{\mathscr{F}}_{o}[\frac{1}{\varpi_{o}}]
is an isomorphism.

Now
{\mathscr{M}}_{o}
is a free right
\M_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
-module of rank
1
. Applying Morita equivalence to the right
\M_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
-action shows that there exists pair
({\mathscr{M}}_{o}^{\prime},{\mathscr{F}}_{o}^{\prime})
satisfying

\displaystyle({\mathscr{M}}_{o},{\mathscr{F}}_{o})=({\mathscr{M}}_{o}^{\prime\oplus n},{\mathscr{F}}_{o}^{\prime\oplus n}),

where
{\mathscr{M}}_{o}^{\prime}
is a locally free
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}[\![\varpi_{o}]\!]
-module of rank
n
, and
{\mathscr{F}}_{o}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\sigma^{*}_{o}{\mathscr{M}}_{o}^{\prime}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{M}}_{o}^{\prime}
is an
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}[\![\varpi_{o}]\!]
-linear morphism whose cokernel is a locally free
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}
-module of rank
1
and whose generic fiber is an isomorphism. Note that if
S
is actually a scheme over
\Spf{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
, then
({\mathscr{M}}_{o}^{\prime},{\mathscr{F}}_{o}^{\prime})
actually forms an effective minuscule local shtuka over
S
of rank
n
and dimension
1
.

5.17.

With the above construction, we can treat level structures in the case when characteristics divide the level. As with abelian varieties, this requires a notion of
Drinfeld level structure
.

Begin by recalling some material from §
1
. We have a notion of finite
\kappa_{o}
-shtukas, which are truncated versions of local shtukas, and they correspond to certain module schemes called strict
\kappa_{o}
-modules under a Dieudonné equivalence. Now the quotient module
{\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}
is a finite
\kappa_{o}
-shtuka, and we denote the corresponding finite
\kappa_{o}
-strict module using
\Dr({\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
. As with local shtukas, we define Drinfeld level structures via using
\Dr(-)
to pass to module schemes.

Definition
.

Let
S
be a scheme over
\Spec{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
, and let
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
be a
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf over
S
. We say a
Drinfeld level-
m
structure
on
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
is a Drinfeld level-
m
structure on
\Dr({\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
, that is, an
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}
-module morphism

\displaystyle\alpha\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}(\varpi^{-m}{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/{\mathcal{O}}_{o})^{n}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\Dr({\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}/\varpi^{m}_{o})(S)

such that the collection of all
\alpha(x)
for
x
in
(\varpi^{-m}{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/{\mathcal{O}}_{o})^{n}
forms a full set of sections of
\Dr({\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}/\varpi^{m}_{o})
as in
[
30
, (1.8.2)]
. For any non-negative integer
m^{\prime}\leq m
and Drinfeld level-
m
structure
\alpha
, its restriction to
(\varpi^{-m^{\prime}}_{o}{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/{\mathcal{O}}_{o})^{n}
is a Drinfeld level-
m^{\prime}
structure.

5.18.

We may finally define moduli spaces of
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves at bad reduction. For any scheme
S
over
\Spec{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
, write
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{*}(S)
for the category whose

\bullet

objects are triples
(({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i},\iota,\alpha)
, where
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
is a
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf over
S
,
\iota
is a level
(I\smallsetminus o)
-structure on
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
, and
\alpha
is a Drinfeld level-
m
structure on
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
, where
m
is the multiplicity of
o
in
I
,

\bullet

morphisms are isomorphisms of pairs.

Then
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{*}
forms an fppf stack over
\kappa
, and when
I
does not contain
o
, we see that
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{*}
is simply the pullback of
{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}
to
\Spec{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
.

By sending a
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf to its zero, we obtain a morphism
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{*}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\Spec{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
. For any closed subscheme
I^{\prime}
of
I
, restriction of level structures gives us a morphism
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{*}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{M}}_{I^{\prime}}^{*}
over
\Spec{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
, and partitioning by degrees allows us to obtain a decomposition

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{*}=\coprod_{d}{\mathcal{M}}_{I,d}^{*}

that respects restriction morphisms, where
d
runs over all integers.

We see that the translation action of
{\mathbb{Z}}
from
5.13
naturally extends to an action on
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{*}
, and we write
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
for the quotient stack
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}\coloneqq{\mathcal{M}}_{I}/{\mathbb{Z}}
. We have the following representability result, which follows from work of Katz–Mazur.

Proposition

(
[
30
, (1.10.13)]
)
.

The restriction morphism
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{*}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{M}}_{I^{\prime}}^{*}
is finite and representable.

By combining this with Proposition
5.13
, we see that
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
is a projective scheme of dimension
n-1
over
\Spec{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
whenever
I\smallsetminus o
is nonempty. The scheme
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
shall be our focus in the next few sections.

5.19.

In this subsection, we extend the Hecke action to
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
. We use
o^{m}
to denote the finite closed subscheme of
C
supported on
o
with multiplicity
m
. Since Drinfeld level-
m
structures over
\mathbf{F}_{o}
are equivalent to level-
o^{m}
structures over
\mathbf{F}_{o}

[
10
, Proposition 7.1.3]
, the generic fiber
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\mathbf{F}_{o}}
is isomorphic to the pullback
{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I,\mathbf{F}_{o}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
. Under this identification, the right action of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}
on
{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},\mathbf{F}_{o}}^{\infty}/a^{\mathbb{Z}}
extends
[
10
, p. 599]
to a right action on the inverse limit

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}^{\infty}\coloneqq\varprojlim_{I}{\mathcal{M}}_{I},

where
I
runs over all finite closed subschemes of
C\smallsetminus\infty
. This action satisfies
{\mathcal{M}}^{\infty}/{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty}={\mathcal{M}}_{I}
. Furthermore, for any
g
in
D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty}
, the correspondence from
5.15
extends to a correspondence
[
10
, p. 600]

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\mathcal{M}}^{\infty}/({\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty}\cap g^{-1}{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty}g)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{1,{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}}
\scriptstyle{c_{2,{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}^{\infty}/{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}^{\infty}/{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty}}

where
c_{1,{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}
and
c_{2,{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}
are finite morphisms over
\Spec{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
. We write
c_{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{M}}_{I}\dashrightarrow{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{\infty}
for the correspondence formed by
c_{1,{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}
and
c_{2,{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}
.

5.20.

Next, we present the
Serre–Tate theorem
for
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves, which relates deformations of
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves with deformations of their associated local shtukas. Let
m
be the multiplicity of
o
in
I
, let
z
be a
\overline{\kappa}
-point of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
, and let
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
be the corresponding
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf over
\overline{\kappa}
with level
(I\smallsetminus o)
-structure
\iota
and Drinfeld level-
m
structure
\alpha
. Then
\alpha
yields a Drinfeld level-
m
structure for the local shtuka
{\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}
formed in
5.16
. Recall from
1.4
that
{\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}
corresponds to some
\delta_{o}
in

\displaystyle\GL_{n}(\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o})\diag(\varpi_{o},1,\dotsc,1)\GL_{n}(\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o})

uniquely up to
\GL_{n}(\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o})
-
\sigma_{o}
-conjugacy, and recall that the deformation space of
({\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o},\alpha)
is
\breve{\mathfrak{X}}_{\delta_{o},\alpha}
. Write
({\mathcal{M}}_{I,\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}})_{z}^{\wedge}
for the completion of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}
at
z
. Boyer proves the following comparison result for deformation spaces.

Proposition

(
[
10
, Theorem 7.4.4]
)
.

Our
(({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i},\iota,\alpha)\mapsto({\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o},\alpha)
induces an isomorphism
({\mathcal{M}}_{I,\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}})_{z}^{\wedge}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}\breve{\mathfrak{X}}_{\delta_{o},\alpha}
over
\Spf\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
that preserves the right action of
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
as well as the Weil action.

5.21.

We extend the above results to
\breve{\mathfrak{X}}_{\delta_{o},m}
, where
\breve{\mathfrak{X}}_{\delta_{o},m}
is the deformation space of
{\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}
along with a Drinfeld level-
m
structure as in
1.12
. Write
\pi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}
for the restriction morphism. We see that
\pi^{-1}(\pi(z))
corresponds to all possible Drinfeld level-
m
structures on
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
. Therefore the isomorphism in Proposition
5.20
also induces an isomorphism
({\mathcal{M}}_{I,\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}})^{\wedge}_{\pi^{-1}(\pi(z))}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}\breve{\mathfrak{X}}_{\delta_{o},m}
over
\Spf\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
, where
({\mathcal{M}}_{I,\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}})^{\wedge}_{\pi^{-1}(\pi(z))}
is the completion of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}
along
\pi^{-1}(\pi(z))
, and this isomorphism preserves the right action of
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
as well as the Weil action.

5.22.

We conclude this section by discussing a variant of the construction from
5.16
. Let
S
be a scheme over
\Spec{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
, let
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
be a
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf over
S
, let
x
be a closed point in
C\smallsetminus o
, and let
f\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}S\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}C
be a morphism over
\kappa
whose image is
x
.

Write
\Gamma_{f}
for the image of the graph of
f
in
C\times_{\kappa}S
, and denote completions along
\Gamma_{f}
using
(-)^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{f}}
. Then
({\mathscr{E}}_{1})_{\Gamma_{f}}^{\wedge}
is a vector bundle on
(C\times_{\kappa}S)_{\Gamma_{f}}^{\wedge}
of rank
n^{2}
with a right action of
{\mathscr{D}}_{x}
. The adic generic fibers
(j_{1})^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{f},\mathbf{F}_{x}}
and
(t_{1})^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{f},\mathbf{F}_{x}}
as in
[
7
, p. 370]
are isomorphisms, so the composed morphism

\displaystyle(j_{1})^{\wedge,-1}_{\Gamma_{f},\mathbf{F}_{x}}\circ(t_{1})^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{f},\mathbf{F}_{x}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}({}^{\sigma}\!{\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{f},\mathbf{F}_{x}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}({\mathscr{E}}_{1})^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{f},\mathbf{F}_{x}}

is also an isomorphism. Similarly to
5.16
, we may identify
(C\times_{\kappa}S)^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{f}}
with the locally ringed space whose support is
{\lvert S\rvert}
and whose structure sheaf is
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}[\![\varpi_{x}]\!]

[
1
, Lemma 5.3]
. From this point of view, the pair

\displaystyle(({\mathscr{E}}_{1})_{\Gamma_{f}}^{\wedge},(j_{1})^{\wedge,-1}_{\Gamma_{f},\mathbf{F}_{x}}\circ(t_{1})^{\wedge}_{\Gamma_{f},\mathbf{F}_{x}})

corresponds to a pair
({\mathscr{M}}_{x},{\mathscr{F}}_{x})
, where
{\mathscr{M}}_{x}
is a locally free
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}[\![\varpi_{x}]\!]
-module of rank
n^{2}
with a right
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}[\![\varpi_{x}]\!]
-linear action of
{\mathscr{D}}_{x}
, and
{\mathscr{F}}_{x}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\sigma^{*}_{x}{\mathscr{M}}_{x}[\frac{1}{\varpi_{x}}]\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}{\mathscr{M}}_{x}[\textstyle\frac{1}{\varpi_{x}}]
is an
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}(\!(\varpi_{x})\!)
-linear isomorphism that commutes with the right
{\mathscr{D}}_{x}
-action. Note that
{\mathscr{M}}_{x}
is a free right
{\mathscr{D}}_{x}
-module of rank
1
.

When
x
does not equal
\infty
, the graph
\Gamma_{f}
lies away from both
o\times_{\kappa}S
and
\infty\times_{\kappa}S
, so the completions
(t_{1})_{\Gamma_{f}}^{\wedge}
and
(j_{1})_{\Gamma_{f}}^{\wedge}
are isomorphisms. In this case, the morphism
{\mathscr{F}}_{x}
is the localization of an
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}[\![\varpi_{x}]\!]
-module isomorphism
\sigma^{*}_{x}{\mathscr{M}}_{x}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}{\mathscr{M}}_{x}
, which we also refer to using
{\mathscr{F}}_{x}
by abuse of notation.

Finally, in the case when
x
equals
\infty
, we have
{\mathscr{D}}_{\infty}=\M_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{\infty})
because
D
splits at
\infty
. Applying Morita equivalence to the right
\M_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{\infty})
-action on
{\mathscr{M}}_{\infty}
provides a pair
({\mathscr{M}}_{\infty}^{\prime},{\mathscr{F}}_{\infty}^{\prime})
that satisfies

\displaystyle({\mathscr{M}}_{\infty},{\mathscr{F}}_{\infty})=({\mathscr{M}}_{\infty}^{\prime\oplus n},{\mathscr{F}}_{\infty}^{\prime\oplus n}),

where
{\mathscr{M}}_{\infty}^{\prime}
is a locally free
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}[\![\varpi_{\infty}]\!]
-module of rank
n
, and
{\mathscr{F}}_{\infty}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\sigma_{\infty}^{*}{\mathscr{M}}_{\infty}^{\prime}[\frac{1}{\varpi_{\infty}}]\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}{\mathscr{M}}_{\infty}^{\prime}[\textstyle\frac{1}{\varpi_{\infty}}]
is an
{\mathscr{O}}_{S}(\!(\varpi_{\infty})\!)
-linear isomorphism. Furthermore, if
S
is the spectrum of
\overline{\kappa}
, then the isogeny class of
{\mathscr{M}}_{\infty}^{\prime}
has slope
-\frac{1}{n}

[
35
, (9.8).(i)]
under the Dieudonné–Manin classification
[
34
, (2.4.5)]
.

6.
A nearby cycles calculation and semisimple trace

Write
\mathbf{F}_{o,r}
for the
r
-th degree unramified extension of
\mathbf{F}_{o}
, and recall our test function
\phi_{\tau,h}
in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o,r}))
as in §
1
. Our goal in this section is to express the integral

\displaystyle\int_{\sigma_{o}^{-r}I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}\!\dif\tau\,\phi_{\tau,h}

in terms of representations of
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
. We shall use this description in §
9
to show that the preimage of any unramified representation under
\pi\mapsto\rec\pi
remains unramified, which plays an important role in our proof that
\rec
is bijective.

To begin, we present a geometric calculation of the inertia invariants of nearby cycles, due to Scholze. This calculation relies on a case of
Grothendieck’s purity conjecture
as proved by Thomason, which describes relative cohomology in the étale setting. By using results from §
1
, we verify that the hypotheses of this calculation apply to
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
as well as explicitly compute the inertia invariants in terms of representations of
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
, where
m
denotes the multiplicity of
o
in
I
. We then relate nearby cycles to
semisimple trace
, which is a better-behaved variant of Frobenius traces for ramified representations of
W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
. Finally, we conclude by applying results from §
1
once more to describe our integral in terms of semisimple traces.

6.1.

We start by introducing notation for the calculation of nearby cycles. Let
{\mathcal{X}}
be a scheme over
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
of finite type, and write

\displaystyle\overline{\char 17\relax}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{X}}_{\breve{\mathbf{F}}_{o}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{X}}_{\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}\mbox{ and }\overline{\char 16\relax}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{X}}_{\overline{\kappa}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{X}}_{\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}

for the inclusion morphisms of the generic and special fibers of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}
, respectively. Denote the derived
I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
-invariants functor by
R_{I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}
, and denote the derived nearby cycles functor on
{\mathcal{X}}_{\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}
by
R\Psi_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\breve{\mathbf{F}}_{o}}}
. The Galois description of étale sheaves implies that

\displaystyle(R_{I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}R\Psi_{{\mathcal{X}}_{\breve{\mathbf{F}}_{o}}})(-)=\overline{\char 16\relax}^{*}R\overline{\char 17\relax}_{*}((-)_{\breve{\mathbf{F}}_{o}})

as functors from
D^{b}_{c}({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathbf{F}_{o}},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})
to
D^{b}_{c}({\mathcal{X}}_{\kappa_{o}},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})
, since the non-derived versions of both sides are equal.

6.2.

Next, we use the combinatorics of the geometry of
{\mathcal{X}}
to construct a certain family of
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
-vector spaces involved in the calculation of nearby cycles. Assume that
{\mathcal{X}}
is regular, and suppose that the morphism
{\mathcal{X}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\Spec{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
is flat of relative dimension
d
. Suppose we have a
stratification
of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\kappa_{o}}
, i.e. assume that
{\mathcal{X}}_{\kappa_{o}}
can be written as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{X}}_{\kappa_{o}}=\bigcup_{j}\mathring{\mathcal{Z}}_{j},

where
j
ranges over some finite indexing set
{\mathbb{J}}
, and the
\mathring{\mathcal{Z}}_{j}
are disjoint irreducible locally closed subsets of
{\mathcal{X}}_{\kappa_{o}}
whose closures
{\mathcal{Z}}_{j}\coloneqq\overline{\mathring{\mathcal{Z}}_{j}}
are regular and equal to unions of
\mathring{\mathcal{Z}}_{k}
for some
k
. For any
j
in
{\mathbb{J}}
, write
c(j)
for the codimension of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{j}
in
{\mathcal{X}}
, which is positive because
{\mathcal{Z}}_{j}
lies in
{\mathcal{X}}_{\kappa_{o}}
. We write
k\succ j
if
{\mathcal{Z}}_{k}
strictly contains
{\mathcal{Z}}_{j}
.

By inducting on
c(j)
, we shall assign a finite-dimensional
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
-vector space
W_{j}
to any
j
in
{\mathbb{J}}
as follows. In the
c(j)=1
case, set
W_{j}\coloneqq\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
. In the
c(j)=2
case, we let

\displaystyle W_{j}\coloneqq\ker\left(\bigoplus_{k}W_{k}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right),

where
k
runs over elements of
{\mathbb{J}}
satisfying
k\succ j
and
c(k)=1
, and the maps in the direct sum are the identity morphisms. Thus
W_{j}
is just the kernel of the summation map. Note that we have a morphism
W_{j}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}W_{k}
given by

\displaystyle W_{j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow}}\bigoplus_{k}W_{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow}}W_{k}

for all
k\succ j
with
c(k)=1
. Finally, in the
c(j)\geq 3
case, set

\displaystyle W_{j}\coloneqq\ker\left(\bigoplus_{k}W_{k}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{l}W_{l}\right),

where now
k
runs over all elements of
{\mathbb{J}}
satisfying
k\succ j
and
c(k)=c(j)-1
,
l
runs over all elements of
{\mathbb{J}}
satisfying
l\succ j
and
c(l)=c(j)-2
, and the maps in the direct sum are of the form
W_{k}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}W_{l}
for
l\succ k
. Note that we have a morphism
W_{j}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}W_{k}
as before via inclusion and projection, so we may indeed inductively continue this construction.

Let
G
be a group, and suppose it acts on
{\mathcal{X}}_{\kappa_{o}}
in a manner preserving the stratification. Then the stabilizer of
{\mathcal{Z}}_{j}
in
G
acts from the left on
W_{j}
via permuting the
{\mathcal{Z}}_{k}
and hence
W_{k}
for which
k\succ j
.

6.3.

We now present Scholze’s calculation of the inertia invariants of nearby cycles. Let
j
be in
{\mathbb{J}}
. Consider the chain complex

\displaystyle 0\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}W_{j}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{k_{1}}W_{k_{1}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{k_{2}}W_{k_{2}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\dotsb\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{k_{c(j)-1}}W_{k_{c(j)-1}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}0,

where
k_{s}
runs over elements of
{\mathbb{J}}
satisfying
k_{s}\succ j
and
c(k_{s})=c(j)-s
, and the maps in the direct sum are of the form
W_{k_{s}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}W_{k_{s+1}}
for
k_{s+1}\succ k_{s}
. Note that this complex is exact at
W_{j}
by construction. For any
\kappa_{o}
-point
z
of
{\mathcal{X}}
, write
\overline{z}
for the corresponding
\overline{\kappa}
-point of
{\mathcal{X}}
.

Proposition

(
[
42
, Theorem 5.3]
)
.

If the above chain complex is exact for all
j
in
{\mathbb{J}}
, then we have a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle(\overline{\char 16\relax}^{*}R^{i}\overline{\char 17\relax}_{*}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})_{\overline{z}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}\bigoplus_{k}W_{k}(-i)

for all
\kappa_{o}
-points
z
of
{\mathcal{X}}
and non-negative integers
i
, where
k
ranges over all
k
in
{\mathbb{J}}
such that
z
lies in
{\mathcal{Z}}_{k}
and
c(k)=i
.

The proof of this proposition uses a case of
Grothendieck’s purity conjecture
as proved by Thomason
[
47
, Corollary 3.9]
, which says that relative étale cohomology is concentrated in the expected degree according to codimension.

6.4.

In order to apply Proposition
6.3
, we must first describe a stratification of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}
as in
6.2
. This stratification will be defined in terms of Drinfeld level-
m
structures, and it is called the
Newton stratification
.

Let
V
be an
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}
-linear direct summand of
(\varpi_{o}^{-m}{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/{\mathcal{O}}_{o})^{n}
, and write
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
for the subfunctor of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
consisting of triples
(({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i},\iota,\alpha)
that satisfy

\displaystyle\sum_{v}[\alpha(v)]=\#V\cdot[0],

where
v
runs over all elements of
V
,
[0]
denotes the zero section of
\Dr({\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
, the sum is taken as closed subschemes of
\Dr({\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
, and
\#V\cdot[0]
denotes
\#V
-fold sum of
[0]
as a closed subscheme of
\Dr({\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
. Here,
{\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}
denotes the local shtuka of
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
at
o
as in
5.16
, and
\Dr({\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}/\varpi^{m}_{o})
denotes the strict
\kappa_{o}
-module associated with the finite
\kappa_{o}
-shtuka
{\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}/\varpi^{m}_{o}
under the Dieudonné equivalence
[
21
, Theorem 5.2]
.

6.5 Proposition
.

(i)

The subfunctor
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
is a regular closed subscheme of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
that is equidimensional of dimension
n-\rk_{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi^{m}_{o}}(V)
.

(ii)

The special fiber
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}
equals
\bigcup_{V}{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
, where
V
ranges over all nonzero
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}
-linear direct summands of
(\varpi_{o}^{-m}{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/{\mathcal{O}}_{o})^{n}
.

(iii)

For any
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi^{m}_{o}
-linear direct summand
V^{\prime}
of
(\varpi^{-m}_{o}{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/{\mathcal{O}}_{o})^{n}
, if any irreducible component of
{\mathcal{M}}^{V^{\prime}}_{I}
lies in
{\mathcal{M}}^{V}_{I}
, then
V^{\prime}
contains
V
. Conversely, if
V^{\prime}
contains
V
, then
{\mathcal{M}}^{V^{\prime}}_{I}
lies in
{\mathcal{M}}^{V}_{I}
.

Hence we obtain a decomposition

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}=\bigcup_{V}\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V},

where
V
runs over all nonzero
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}
-linear direct summands of
(\varpi_{o}^{-m}{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/{\mathcal{O}}_{o})^{n}
, and the
\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
are the disjoint locally closed subschemes

\displaystyle\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}\coloneqq{\mathcal{M}}^{V}_{I}\smallsetminus\bigcup_{V^{\prime}}{\mathcal{M}}^{V^{\prime}}_{I},

where
V^{\prime}
runs over all
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}
-linear direct summands of
(\varpi_{o}^{-m}{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/{\mathcal{O}}_{o})^{n}
that strictly contain
V
. Note that the only condition in
6.2
that this decomposition of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}
does not satisfy is that the
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
might not be connected. Furthermore, we see that the right action of
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
on
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}
given by composition with Drinfeld level-
m
structures preserves this decomposition.

Proof of Proposition
6.5
.

The second part of (iii) is immediate. And when
V
is zero,
{\mathcal{M}}^{V}_{I}
equals all of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
, so the whole proposition for this
V
follows immediately. Now suppose
V
is nonzero. Let
\overline{z}
be a geometric point of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
, and write
(({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i},\iota,\alpha)
for the corresponding triple. If
\overline{z}
lay inside the generic fiber
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\mathbf{F}_{o}}
, then
\Dr({\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
would be étale
[
10
, Proposition 7.1.3]
and hence could not possibly satisfy the relation required for
\overline{z}
to lie in
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
. Therefore
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
is contained in
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}
. As the
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
cover
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
as
V
varies, this proves part (ii).

We can check parts (i) and (iii) by passing to the completion of closed points, so now assume that
\overline{z}
is a
\overline{\kappa}
-point. Write
\delta_{o}
for the element of

\displaystyle\GL_{n}(\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o})\diag(\varpi_{o},1,\dotsc,1)\GL_{n}(\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}),

unique up to
\GL_{n}(\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o})
-
\sigma_{o}
-conjugacy, which corresponds to
{\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}
. Then the completion of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
at
\overline{z}
is isomorphic to
\breve{\mathfrak{X}}_{\delta_{o},\alpha}
by Proposition
5.20
. Recall from Proposition
1.10
that the restriction morphism induces an isomorphism
\breve{R}_{\delta_{o}^{\circ},\alpha^{\circ}}[\![T_{1},\dotsc,T_{n-k}]\!]\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}\breve{R}_{\delta_{o},\alpha}
, where
k
is the height of the connected part
{\mathscr{M}}_{o}^{\prime\circ}
of
{\mathscr{M}}_{o}^{\prime}
,
\delta_{o}^{\circ}
is an element of
\GL_{k}(\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o})\diag(\varpi_{o},1,\dotsc,1)\GL_{k}(\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o})
corresponding to
{\mathscr{M}}_{o}^{\prime\circ}
,
9
9

9

We apologize for the notation.
and
\alpha^{\circ}
is the restriction of
\alpha
to
\ker\alpha
. The condition defining
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
depends only on
{\mathscr{M}}_{o}^{\prime\circ}
, so we may further narrow our focus to the local deformation ring
\breve{R}_{\delta_{o}^{\circ},\alpha^{\circ}}
.

Let
e_{1},\dotsc,e_{s}
be an
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}
-basis of
V
. If
\overline{z}
lies in
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
, then
\ker\alpha
contains
V
, so here we may extend this to an
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}
-basis
e_{1},\dotsc,e_{k}
of
\ker\alpha
. For any local Artinian
\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
-algebra
A
with residue field
\overline{\kappa}
and local
\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
-algebra morphism
f\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\breve{R}_{\delta_{o}^{\circ},\alpha^{\circ}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}A
, write
(H^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime},\iota^{\prime})
for the corresponding triple. Recall from Proposition
1.11
that
\breve{R}_{\delta_{o}^{\circ},\alpha^{\circ}}
has a choice of local parameters

such that, for all such

and

X_{i}
under

equals the element of
{\mathfrak{m}}_{A}
corresponding to
\alpha^{\prime}(e_{i})
. Now the condition defining
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\prod_{v}(T-\alpha^{\prime}(v))=(T)^{\#V}

as ideals in the formal power series ring
A[\![T]\!]
, where
v
runs over all elements in
V
. This is equivalent to the condition that
X_{1}=\dotsb=X_{j}=0
. This proves the first part of (iii), and as
X_{1},\dotsc,X_{k}
is a regular sequence, this also proves part (i).
∎

6.6.

The decomposition of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}
from Proposition
6.5
consists of closed subschemes
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
which might not be connected. We can immediately rectify this issue (which is necessary for applying Proposition
6.3
) by refining this decomposition into its irreducible components. To simplify the exposition, we proceed as follows.

Let
z
be a
\kappa_{o}
-point of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
, and write
\overline{z}
for the corresponding
\overline{\kappa}
-point of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
. Then
z
corresponds to a
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
with Drinfeld level-
m
structure
\alpha
. Note that, for any
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}
-linear direct summand
V
of
(\varpi_{o}^{-m}{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/{\mathcal{O}}_{o})^{n}
, the closed subscheme
{\mathcal{M}}^{V}_{I}
contains
z
if and only if
V
lies in
\ker\alpha
.

Given such a
V
, write
\mathring{\mathcal{M}}^{V,0}_{I},\dotsc,\mathring{\mathcal{M}}^{V,l_{V}}_{I}
for the irreducible components of
\mathring{\mathcal{M}}^{V}_{I}
, and write
{\mathcal{M}}^{V,0}_{I},\dotsc,{\mathcal{M}}^{V,l_{V}}_{I}
for their closures, respectively. These are the irreducible components of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
, and regularity implies that they are disjoint. We label them such that
{\mathcal{M}}^{V,0}_{I}
is the one containing
z
. For any
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}
-linear direct summand
V^{\prime}
lying in
V
, the disjointness of the
{\mathcal{M}}^{V^{\prime},0}_{I},\dotsc,{\mathcal{M}}^{V^{\prime},l_{V^{\prime}}}_{I}
implies that precisely one of them contains
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V,a}
, where
a
is any integer
0\leq a\leq l_{V}
. Therefore

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}^{z}\coloneqq\bigcup_{(V,a)}\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V,a},

where
(V,a)
runs over all pairs for which

\bullet

V
is a nonzero
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi^{m}_{o}
-linear direct summand of
(\varpi_{o}^{-m}{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/{\mathcal{O}}_{o})^{n}
such that
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
contains
z
,

\bullet

a
is an integer
0\leq a\leq l_{V}
,

is an open subscheme of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}
. Furthermore, the
\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V,a}
now form a stratification of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}^{z}
as in
6.2
, where the indexing set equals the collection of such pairs
(V,a)
. Proposition
6.5
shows that
c(V,a)=\rk_{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}}(V)
. Note that the right action of
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
on
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}
restricts to an action on
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}^{z}
that preserves this stratification.

6.7.

At this point, we specialize the general constructions from
6.2
to our specific situation of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}^{z}
. Let
W_{V,a}
be the
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
-vector space associated with
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V,a}
as in
6.2
, and make a change of basis in
(\varpi_{o}^{-m}{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/{\mathcal{O}}_{o})^{n}
to identify
V
with
(\varpi_{o}^{-m}{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/{\mathcal{O}}_{o})^{k}
, where
k\leq n
is rank of
V
as a free
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}
-module. Then the stabilizer of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V,a}
under the
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
-action is
P({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
, where
P
denotes the standard parabolic subgroup of
\GL_{n}
with block sizes
(k,n-k)
, so
W_{V,a}
has a left action by the finite group
P({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
.

Proposition
6.5
.(iii) implies that the
{\mathcal{M}}^{V^{\prime},a^{\prime}}_{I}
containing
{\mathcal{M}}^{V,a}_{I}
are precisely those for which
V^{\prime}
lies in
V
. Furthermore, after choosing such a
V^{\prime}
, the integer
a^{\prime}
is uniquely determined by this containment condition. By making another change of basis, we may identify
V^{\prime}
with
(\varpi_{o}^{-m}{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/{\mathcal{O}}_{o})^{k^{\prime}}
, where
k^{\prime}\leq k
is the rank of
V^{\prime}
as a free
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}
-module. Then
Q({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
is the stabilizer of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V^{\prime},a^{\prime}}
under the action of
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
, where
Q
denotes the standard parabolic subgroup of
\GL_{n}
with block sizes
(k^{\prime},n-k^{\prime})
. Note that
Q
contains the unipotent radical of
P
as well as the Levi factor
\GL_{n-k}
of
P
, so we see that
P({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
acts on
W_{V,a}
through the quotient
\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
.

6.8.

Now
6.7
shows that
{\mathcal{M}}^{V,a}_{I}
is contained in a unique irreducible component of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V^{\prime}}
whenever
V^{\prime}
lies in
V
. Therefore, by inducting on
k
and examining the construction of
W_{V,a}
, we see that
W_{V,a}
is isomorphic to the Steinberg representation

\displaystyle\St_{k}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}}\coloneqq\ker\left(\Ind_{B({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{P}\Ind_{P({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)

of
\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})
, where
B
denotes the standard Borel subgroup of
\GL_{k}
of upper triangular matrices,
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
denotes the trivial representation, and
P
ranges over all standard parabolic subgroups of
\GL_{k}
that do not equal
B
. Under this identification, the chain complex from Proposition
6.3
formed from the
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V,a}
becomes

\displaystyle 0\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\St_{k}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\Ind_{P_{k-1}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}\left(\St_{k-1}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}}\otimes\,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\Ind_{P_{k-2}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}\left(\St_{k-2}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}}\otimes\,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\dotsb

\displaystyle\dotsb

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\Ind_{P_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}\left(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\otimes\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}0,

where
P_{s}
denotes the standard parabolic subgroup of
\GL_{k}
with block sizes
(s,k-s)
.

6.9.

We proceed to verify that the chain complex in
6.8
is exact, that is, the hypotheses of Proposition
6.3
hold for the stratification
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}^{z}=\bigcup_{(V,a)}\mathring{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V,a}
.

Lemma
.

For all positive integers
k
, the chain complex

\displaystyle 0\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\St_{k}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\Ind_{P_{k-1}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}\left(\St_{k-1}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}}\otimes\,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\Ind_{P_{k-2}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}\left(\St_{k-2}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}}\otimes\,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\dotsb

\displaystyle\dotsb

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\Ind_{P_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}\left(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\otimes\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}0,

is exact.

In the proof, we will frequently pass between induced representations of
\GL_{k}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
and
\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
. For this, one can use the fact that
P({\mathcal{O}}_{o})\backslash\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
equals
P(\mathbf{F}_{o})\backslash\GL_{k}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
to show that

\displaystyle\Ind_{P(\mathbf{F}_{o})}^{\GL_{k}(\mathbf{F}_{o})}\lambda=\Ind_{P({\mathcal{O}}_{o})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})}\lambda

as representations of
\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
, where
P
is a parabolic subgroup of
\GL_{k}
over
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
, and
\lambda
is a smooth representation of
P(\mathbf{F}_{o})
.

Proof of Lemma
6.9
.

We reduce this to a fact about representations of
\GL_{k}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
as follows. Suppose we could show that the chain complex

\displaystyle 0\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\St_{k}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\nInd_{P_{k-1}(\mathbf{F}_{o})}^{\GL_{k}(\mathbf{F}_{o})}\left(\St_{k-1}\otimes\,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\nInd_{P_{k-2}(\mathbf{F}_{o})}^{\GL_{k}(\mathbf{F}_{o})}\left(\St_{k-2}\otimes\,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\dotsb

\displaystyle\dotsb

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\nInd_{P_{1}(\mathbf{F}_{o})}^{\GL_{k}(\mathbf{F}_{o})}\left(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\otimes\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}0,

is exact, where
\St_{s}
is the Steinberg representation of
\GL_{s}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
, that is, the representation
\St_{s}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})
in the terminology of Definition
2.3
. As the modulus character
\delta_{P_{s}}
of
P_{s}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
vanishes on
P_{s}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
, we see that the above chain complex is isomorphic as representations of
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
to

\displaystyle 0\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\St_{k}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\Ind_{P_{k-1}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})}\left(\St_{k-1}\otimes\,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\Ind_{P_{k-2}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})}\left(\St_{k-2}\otimes\,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\dotsb

\displaystyle\dotsb

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\Ind_{P_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})}\left(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\otimes\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}0,

which is therefore also exact.

Subgroups of
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}
-points are compact, so modulus characters vanish on them. Therefore we have

\displaystyle\St_{s}=\ker\left(\nInd_{B(\mathbf{F}_{o})}^{\GL_{s}(\mathbf{F}_{o})}\delta_{B}^{1/2}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{P}\nInd_{P(\mathbf{F}_{o})}^{\GL_{s}(\mathbf{F}_{o})}\delta_{P}^{1/2}\right)=\ker\left(\Ind_{B({\mathcal{O}}_{o})}^{\GL_{s}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{P}\Ind_{P({\mathcal{O}}_{o})}^{\GL_{s}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)

as representations of
\GL_{s}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
, where
B
denotes the standard Borel subgroup of
\GL_{s}
,
P
runs over all standard parabolic subgroups of
\GL_{b}
that do not equal
B
, and
\delta_{B}
and
\delta_{P}
denote the modulus characters, where we have identified
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
with
{\mathbb{C}}
. Thus taking
(1+\varpi_{o}^{m}\M_{s}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}))
-invariants of
\St_{s}
yields
\St_{s}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}}
. Because the Peter–Weyl theorem implies that taking
(1+\varpi_{o}^{m}\M_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}))
-invariants preserves exactness, we conclude that

\displaystyle 0\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\St_{k}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\Ind_{P_{k-1}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}\left(\St_{k-1}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}}\otimes\,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\Ind_{P_{k-2}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}\left(\St_{k-2}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}}\otimes\,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\dotsb

\displaystyle\dotsb

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\Ind_{P_{1}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}\left(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\otimes\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}0

is exact, as desired.

Therefore it suffices to show that our chain complex of representations of
\GL_{k}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
is exact. A routine calculation
[
20
, Lemma I.3.2]
using the graph-theoretic description of the Jordan–Hölder factors of

\displaystyle\Ind_{P_{s}(\mathbf{F}_{o})}^{\GL_{k}(\mathbf{F}_{o})}\left(\St_{s}\otimes\,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)

shows that there exists irreducible smooth representations
\pi_{0},\dotsc,\pi_{k-1}
of
\GL_{k}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
such that the above representation has
\pi_{s}
as a subrepresentation and
\pi_{s-1}
as a quotient. Because the maps in our chain complex are nonzero, this fact indicates that our chain complex of representations of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
is exact, which concludes the proof.
∎

6.10.

With Lemma
6.9
in hand, we may apply Proposition
6.3
to our situation. This finally allows us to compute the inertia invariants of nearby cycles on
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
.

Corollary
.

Write
k
for the rank of
\ker\alpha
as a free
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}
-module. We have a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle(R_{I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}^{i}R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\breve{\mathbf{F}}_{o}}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})_{\overline{z}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}\begin{cases}\Ind_{P_{i}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}\left(\St_{i}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}}\otimes\,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)(-i)&\mbox{ if }0\leq i\leq k,\\
0&\mbox{otherwise},\end{cases}

as representations of
\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})\times W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
, where
P_{i}
denotes the standard parabolic subgroup of
\GL_{k}
with block sizes
(i,k-i)
.

Proof.

Because
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}^{z}
is an open subscheme of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}
and stalks are local, we may replace
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}
with its open subscheme
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\kappa_{o}}^{z}\cup{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\mathbf{F}_{o}}
. In
6.1
, we saw that

\displaystyle R_{I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}^{i}R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\breve{\mathbf{F}}_{o}}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}=\overline{\char 16\relax}^{*}R^{i}\overline{\char 17\relax}_{*}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell},

and Lemma
6.9
implies that we may compute
\overline{\char 16\relax}^{*}R^{i}\overline{\char 17\relax}_{*}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
by means of Proposition
6.3
. Recall that
z
lies in
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}^{V}
if and only if
V
lies in
\ker\alpha
. Therefore the same argument as in
6.8
shows that the right hand side of Proposition
6.3
yields

\displaystyle\Ind_{P_{i}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}^{\GL_{k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})}\left(\St_{i}^{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}}\otimes\,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right)(-i),

if
0\leq i\leq k
, as desired, while we get zero otherwise because
\ker\alpha
contains no
{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m}
-linear direct summands of rank
i>k
.
∎

6.11.

At this point, we turn to
semisimple traces
. Let
H
be a finite group, and let
Y
be a finite-dimensional continuous representation of
H\times W_{\mathbf{F}_{o,r}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
. Because taking
I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
-invariants is not exact, the operation
Y\mapsto\tr(\sigma_{o}^{-r}|Y^{I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}})
is not additive. One way of rectifying this starts by passing to certain filtrations of
Y
.

Definition
.

An exhaustive filtration

\displaystyle 0=Y_{0}\subseteq Y_{1}\subseteq\dotsb\subseteq Y_{d}=Y

of
H\times W_{\mathbf{F}_{o,r}}
-subrepresentations is
admissible
if
I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
acts through a finite quotient on the associated graded representation

\displaystyle\gr Y_{\bullet}\coloneqq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{d}Y_{i}/Y_{i-1}.

Note that refinements of admissible filtrations remain admissible, as do their sums and intersections.

6.12.

To ensure that admissible filtrations of
Y
exist, we shall use the following version of Grothendieck’s
\ell
-adic monodromy theorem.

Lemma
.

There exists an admissible filtration of
Y
.

Proof.

One can prove this precisely as in
[
41
, Lemma 7.2]
: the argument proceeds as in the usual proof of Grothendieck’s
\ell
-adic monodromy theorem, except we use
W_{\mathbf{F}_{o,r}}
in place of
G_{o}
and carry around the extra commuting action of
H
throughout.

∎

6.13 Definition
.

Let
r
be a non-negative integer, and let
h
be in
H
. The
semisimple trace
of
h\times\sigma^{-r}_{o}
on
Y
is

\displaystyle\tr^{\text{ss}}\left(h\times\sigma^{-r}_{o}|Y\right)\coloneqq\tr\left(h\times\sigma^{-r}_{o}|(\gr Y_{\bullet})^{I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}\right),

where
Y_{\bullet}
is any admissible filtration of
Y
.

Note that the common refinement of two admissible filtrations remains admissible. Taking
I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
-invariants on the associated graded representation is exact because
I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
acts through a finite quotient, so we see that
\tr^{\text{ss}}(h\times\sigma^{-r}_{o}|Y)
is independent of the choice of
Y_{\bullet}
. This same observation shows that
\tr^{\text{ss}}(h\times\sigma^{-r}_{o}|-)
itself is additive. Therefore semisimple trace descends to the derived category of finite-dimensional continuous representations of
H\times W_{\mathbf{F}_{o},r}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
, and it is additive on exact triangles.

6.14.

Taking derived
I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
-invariants is also additive on exact triangles, so we can consider the additive function
\tr(h\times\sigma_{o}^{-r}|-)\circ R_{I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}
as well. It has the following relationship with semisimple trace.

Lemma
.

We have

\displaystyle\tr\left(h\times\sigma_{o}^{-r}|-\right)\circ R_{I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}=(1-q_{o}^{r})\tr^{\text{ss}}\left(h\times\sigma_{o}^{-r}|-\right)

as functions on the derived category of finite-dimensional continuous representations of
H\times W_{\mathbf{F}_{o,r}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
.

Proof.

One can prove this exactly as in
[
41
, Lemma 7.5]
: first, reduce to the case of a complex concentrated in one degree, and take wild inertia invariants. Since wild inertia is a pro-
p
group, while the modules in question are
\ell
-torsion, Maschke’s theorem indicates that this is exact. Next, we take tame inertia invariants: as tame inertia is pro-cyclic, we can take the standard resolution for pro-cyclic groups to conclude the proof.
∎

6.15.

Semisimple traces have the following interpretation in terms of integrals of traces.

Lemma
.

We have an equality

\displaystyle\tr^{\text{ss}}\left(h\times\sigma_{o}^{-r}|Y\right)=\int_{\sigma_{o}^{-r}I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}\!\dif\tau\,\tr\left(h\times\tau|Y\right),

where
\dif\tau
is the Haar measure on
W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
that gives
I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
volume
1
.

Proof.

Because both sides are additive in
Y
, it suffices to prove this when
Y
is irreducible. Lemma
6.12
implies that
I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
acts on
Y
through a finite quotient, so we obtain

\displaystyle\tr^{\text{ss}}\left(h\times\sigma_{o}^{-r}|Y\right)=\tr\left(h\times\sigma_{o}^{-r}|Y^{I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}\right).

Furthermore,
Y
is a smooth representation of
W_{\mathbf{F}_{o,r}}
, and the action of
h\times\sigma_{o}^{-r}
on
Y^{I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}
is given by the action of the function

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{h\times\sigma_{o}^{-r}I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(H\times W_{\mathbf{F}_{o,r}})

on
Y
, where
\mathbf{1}_{h\times\sigma_{o}^{-r}I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}
is the indicator function on
h\times\sigma_{p}^{-r}I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
. The trace of this function is given by the above integral, so we obtain the desired equality.
∎

6.16.

Let
h
be a function in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}))
. We conclude by combining the results in this section to write the integral of
\phi_{\tau,h}
, as
\tau
ranges over elements of
\sigma_{o}^{-r}I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
, in terms of representations of
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
. Write
{\mathcal{O}}_{o,r}
for the ring of integers of
\mathbf{F}_{o,r}
, and write
\kappa_{o,r}
for the residue field of
{\mathcal{O}}_{o,r}
. Let
\delta_{o}
be an element of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o,r})
, and if
\delta_{o}
lies in
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o,r})\diag(\varpi_{o},1,\dotsc,1)\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o,r})
, write
k
for the rank of the connected part of the associated effective minuscule local shtuka
H_{\delta_{o}}
over
\Spec\kappa_{o,r}
as in §
1
.

Corollary
.

The integral

\displaystyle\int_{\sigma_{o}^{-r}I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}\!\dif\tau\,\phi_{\tau,h}(\delta_{o})

vanishes if
\delta_{o}
does not lie in
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o,r})\diag(\varpi_{o},1,\dotsc,1)\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o,r})
. On the other hand, if
\delta_{o}
lies in this double coset, then the above integral equals

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{C}}_{k}\coloneqq\frac{1}{1-q_{o}^{r}}\sum_{i=0}^{k}(-1)^{i}q_{o}^{ir}\tr\left(h|\Ind_{P_{i,k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})}^{\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})}\left(\St_{i}\otimes\,{\mathbb{C}}\right)\right),

where
P_{i,k}
is the standard parabolic subgroup of
\GL_{n}
with block sizes
(i,k-i,n-k)
, and
{\mathbb{C}}
denotes the trivial representation of
\GL_{k-i}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})\times\GL_{n-k}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
.

Proof.

Because
\phi_{\tau,h}(\delta_{o})
vanishes for
\delta_{o}
not in
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o,r})\diag(\varpi_{o},1,\dotsc,1)\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o,r})
by definition, it suffices to prove this when
\delta_{o}
actually does lie in this double coset. Recalling the definition of
\phi_{\tau,h}(\delta_{o})
for such
\delta_{o}
yields

\displaystyle\int_{\sigma_{o}^{-r}I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}\!\dif\tau\,\phi_{\tau,h}(\delta_{o})=\int_{\sigma_{o}^{-r}I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}\!\dif\tau\,\tr\left(\tau\times h|[R\psi_{\delta_{o}}]\right),

where
[R\psi_{\delta_{o}}]
is the virtual
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})\times I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
-admissible/continuous representation as in
[
20
, p. 24]
of
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})\times W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
from
1.15
. Let
m
be a positive integer for which
h
descends to a function in
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}[\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})]
, where we have identified
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
with
{\mathbb{C}}
. We denote this element in
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}[\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})]
using
h
by abuse of notation. Then this integral of traces becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{i}\int_{\sigma_{o}^{-r}I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}\!\dif\tau\,\tr\left(\tau\times h|R^{i}\psi_{\delta_{o},m}\right),

where
R^{i}\psi_{\delta_{o},m}
is the finite-dimensional continuous representation of
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})\times W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
from
1.14
, since

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{i}R^{i}\psi_{\delta_{o},m}

are the
(1+\varpi_{o}^{m}\M_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}))
-invariants of
[R\psi_{\delta_{o}}]
. Lemma
6.15
shows that our above sum of integrals equals

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{i}\tr^{\text{ss}}\left(\sigma_{o}^{-r}\times h|R^{i}\psi_{\delta_{o},m}\right).

Next, Lemma
6.14
implies that this sum of traces equals

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-q_{o}^{r}}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{i}\tr\left(\sigma_{o}^{-r}\times h|R_{I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}R^{i}\psi_{\delta_{o},m}\right).

Using
{\mathcal{M}}_{o^{m},\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}
as an algebraization of
\breve{\mathfrak{X}}_{\delta_{o},m}
as in the proof of Lemma
1.14
, write
z
for the point of
{\mathcal{M}}_{\varnothing,\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}
corresponding to
\delta_{o}
, and write
\pi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{M}}_{o^{m},\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{M}}_{\varnothing,\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}
for the restriction morphism. Then Berkovich’s nearby cycles comparison theorem
[
7
, Theorem 3.1]
shows that the above sum becomes

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-q_{o}^{r}}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{i}\tr\left(\sigma_{o}^{-r}\times h|R^{i}_{I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}\left.R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{o^{m},\breve{\mathbf{F}}_{o}}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right|_{\pi^{-1}(z)}\right),

where now we use the alternating product to expand
R_{I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}
instead of
R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{o^{m},\breve{\mathbf{F}}_{o}}}
. Applying the decomposition of
\breve{\mathfrak{X}}_{\delta_{o},m}
in Proposition
1.12
alongside the calculation of inertia invariants in Corollary
6.10
yields the desired expression, where we have identified
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
with
{\mathbb{C}}
.
∎

7.
Langlands–Kottwitz counting and the Serre–Tate trick

In this section, all representations shall be taken over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
, and we view
{\mathbb{C}}
-valued functions as
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
-valued ones via our fixed identification
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}={\mathbb{C}}
. Let
f_{\tau,h}
be our test function in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o}))
from §
1
, and let
f^{\infty,o}
be any function in
C^{\infty}((D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty,o})^{\times})
. Our goal is to relate the trace of
f^{\infty,o}\times h\times\tau
to the trace of
f^{\infty,o}\times f_{\tau,h}
on the virtual representation
[H_{\xi}]
. We begin by using the nearby cycles spectral sequence to pass from the generic fiber to the special fiber. Then, we use
Deligne’s conjecture
as proven by Fujiwara, which describes this trace in terms of manageable local terms, to convert this into a sum of terms indexed by geometric points in the special fiber. These points correspond to isomorphism classes of
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves with extra structure.

We express this sum in terms of orbital integrals by using the equi-characteristic analog of
Langlands–Kottwitz counting
, which describes isomorphism classes of
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves in terms of the algebraic group-theoretic data associated with their local shtukas. Here, the Serre–Tate theorem from §
5
allows us to convert contributions from
h\times\tau
into contributions from
f_{\tau,h}
. We then use the
Selberg trace formula
to rewrite this expression in terms of automorphic representations. Finally, results of Laumon–Rapoport–Stuhler on the cohomology of
{\mathcal{E}}\ell\ell_{{\mathscr{D}},I}
allow us to conclude.

7.1.

The following identity is the main result of this section. Write
v_{o}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{Z}}
for the canonical surjection that sends geometric
q_{o}
-Frobenius at
o
to
1
in
{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Proposition
.

Let
\tau
be an element
W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
with
v_{o}(\tau)>0
, let
h
be a function in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}))
, and let
f^{\infty,o}
be a function in
C^{\infty}_{c}((D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty,o})^{\times})
. Then we have an equality of traces

\displaystyle\tr(f^{\infty,o}\times h\times\tau|[H_{\xi}])=\textstyle\frac{1}{n}\tr(f^{\infty,o}\times f_{\tau,h}|[H_{\xi}]),

where
f_{\tau,h}
in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o}))
is the function defined as in
1.17
.

Proof.

We start with some immediate reductions. Note that both sides are
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
-linear with respect to the function
f^{\infty,o}\times h
in
C^{\infty}_{c}((D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times})
. Therefore it suffices to prove this equality for

\displaystyle f^{\infty,o}=\frac{1}{\vol({\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty,o})}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathscr{K}}^{\infty,o}_{I}g^{o}{\mathscr{K}}^{\infty,o}_{I}}\mbox{ and }h=\frac{1}{\vol({\mathscr{K}}_{I,o}^{\infty})}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathscr{K}}_{I,o}^{\infty}g_{o}{\mathscr{K}}_{I,o}^{\infty}},

where
I
can be any sufficiently large finite closed subscheme of
C\smallsetminus\infty
, and
g
is an element of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}
for which
g_{o}
lies in
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})
. With these choices of
f^{\infty,o}
and
h
, our trace becomes

\displaystyle\tr(f^{\infty,o}\times h\times\tau|[H_{\xi}])=\tr(c_{\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}}\times\tau|[H_{\xi}]),

where
c_{\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}}=(c_{1,\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}},c_{2,\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}})
is the pullback
[
48
, 1.1.8]
to
\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}
of the correspondence from
5.15
that induces the action of
f^{\infty,o}\times h
. Next, we pass to
{\mathbb{C}}_{o}
and apply nearby cycles, which shows that this trace equals

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{i}\tr\left(c_{\overline{\kappa}}\times\tau|H^{i}({\mathcal{M}}_{I,\overline{\kappa}},R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{\xi,I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}})\right),

where
R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}
denotes the derived nearby cycles functor
[
14
, XIII (1.3.2.3)]
on
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}
, and
c_{\overline{\kappa}}=(c_{1,\overline{\kappa}},c_{2,\overline{\kappa}})
is the pullback to
\overline{\kappa}
of the correspondence
c_{{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}
from
5.19
that extends
c_{\mathbf{F}}
. Writing
\pi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{M}}_{I}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o}
for the restriction morphism, we see that the above sum equals

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{i}\tr\left(\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}}\times\tau|H^{i}({\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\overline{\kappa}},\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{\xi,I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}})\right),

where
\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}}=(\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{1,\overline{\kappa}},\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{1,\overline{\kappa}})
is the pushforward correspondence
[
48
, 1.1.6]
of
c_{\overline{\kappa}}
. Next, write
r=v_{o}(\tau)
. The independence of characters implies that it suffices to prove our desired equalities for sufficiently large
r
. With this reduction in hand, we may now apply Deligne’s conjecture as proven by Fujiwara
[
18
, Corollary 5.4.5]
to obtain the sum

\displaystyle\sum_{y}\tr\left((\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}}\times\tau)_{y}|(\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{\xi,I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}})_{c_{2,\overline{\kappa}}(y)}\right),

where
y
runs over all
\overline{\kappa}
-points of
\Fix(\sigma_{o}^{r}\circ\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}})
, and
\Fix(\sigma_{o}^{r}\circ\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}})
in turn denotes the fiber product

\textstyle{\Fix(\sigma_{o}^{r}\circ c_{\overline{\kappa}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}^{\infty}_{\overline{\kappa}}/({\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty,o}\cap g^{o,-1}{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty,o}g^{o})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\sigma_{o}^{r}\circ\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{1,\overline{\kappa}},\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{2,\overline{\kappa}})}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\overline{\kappa}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o},\overline{\kappa}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\overline{\kappa}}\times_{\overline{\kappa}}{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\overline{\kappa}}}

At this point, we shall use the following lemma to decompose the
\ell
-adic sheaf whose cohomology we are studying.

7.2 Lemma
.

We have an isomorphism in the derived category of constructible
\ell
-adic sheaves

\displaystyle(\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\otimes{\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,\overline{\kappa}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{\xi,I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}

that preserves the actions of
I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
and
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}
.

For the proof of this lemma, we will need the following notation. Write

\displaystyle\overline{\char 17\relax}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}\mbox{ and }\overline{\char 16\relax}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\overline{\kappa}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}

for the inclusion morphisms of the generic and special fibers of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}
, respectively. In the proof of this lemma, we shall freely use the fact that

\displaystyle R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}\pi_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o},*}=\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}},

which follows
[
14
, XIII (1.3.6)]
from finite base change.

Proof of Lemma
7.2
.

We start by identifying

\displaystyle(\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\otimes{\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,\overline{\kappa}}=(R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}\pi_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o},*}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\otimes\overline{\char 16\relax}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}.

Next, applying the projection formula to canonical adjunction morphisms yields a morphism
[
48
, (0.2)]

(
\star
)

\displaystyle(\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\otimes{\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,\overline{\kappa}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\overline{\char 16\relax}^{*}(R\overline{\char 17\relax}_{*}\pi_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o},*}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\otimes R\overline{\char 17\relax}_{*}\overline{\char 17\relax}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}),

and taking
\overline{\char 16\relax}^{*}
of a similar morphism
[
48
, (0.1)]

\displaystyle R\overline{\char 17\relax}_{*}\pi_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o},*}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\otimes R\overline{\char 17\relax}_{*}\overline{\char 17\relax}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}R\overline{\char 17\relax}_{*}(\pi_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o},*}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\otimes\overline{\char 17\relax}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}})

provides another morphism

(
\star\star
)

\displaystyle\overline{\char 16\relax}^{*}(R\overline{\char 17\relax}_{*}\pi_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o},*}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\otimes R\overline{\char 17\relax}_{*}\overline{\char 17\relax}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}})\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}(\pi_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o},*}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}).

By reducing to finite coefficients and then checking on an étale trivialization, we see that the composition of Equation (

⋆

‣
7.1
) and Equation (

⋆
⁣
⋆

‣
7.1
) is an isomorphism. Applying the projection formula
[
4
, XVII (5.2.9)]
to
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
and
{\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}
implies that

\displaystyle R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}(\pi_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o},*}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}})=R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I\smallsetminus o,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}\pi_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o},*}\pi^{*}_{{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}{\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}=\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{\xi,I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}},

as desired.
∎

Return to the proof of Proposition
7.1
. With this tensor product decomposition, our sum of traces becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{y}\tr\left((\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}}\times\tau)_{y}|(\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})_{c_{2,\overline{\kappa}}(y)}\right)\tr\left((\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}}\times\tau)_{y}|({\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,\overline{\kappa}})_{c_{2,\overline{\kappa}}(y)}\right).

Write
z
for
c_{2,\overline{\kappa}}(y)
, write
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
for the
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf over
\overline{\kappa}
corresponding to
z
, write
{\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}
for its local shtuka at
o
as in
5.16
, and write
\delta_{o}
for the corresponding element of the double coset

\displaystyle\langle\varpi_{o}\rangle\coloneqq\GL_{n}(\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o})\diag(\varpi_{o},1,\dotsc,1)\GL_{n}(\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}),

which is unique up to
\GL_{n}(\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o})
-
\sigma_{o}
-conjugacy. Because
y
lies in
\Fix(\sigma_{o}^{r}\circ\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}})
, we see
z
is defined over
\kappa_{r}
, so
\delta_{o}
can naturally be chosen to lie in
\langle\varpi_{o}\rangle\cap\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o,r})
.

Writing
m
for the multiplicity of
o
in
I
, we see that finite base change implies that the above sum equals

\displaystyle\sum_{y}\tr\left(c_{\overline{\kappa}}\times\tau|H^{i}(\pi^{-1}(z),\left.R\Psi_{{\mathcal{M}}_{I,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\right|_{\pi^{-1}(z)})\right)\tr\left((\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}}\times\tau)_{y}|({\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,\overline{\kappa}})_{z}\right).

Because
\breve{\mathfrak{X}}_{\delta_{o},m}
is the completion of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I,\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}}
along
\pi^{-1}(z)
by
5.21
, Berkovich’s nearby cycles comparison theorem
[
7
, Theorem 3.1]
indicates that this equals

\displaystyle\sum_{y}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\tr\left(g_{o}\times\tau|H^{i}({\mathfrak{X}}_{\delta_{o},m,\overline{\kappa}},R^{i}\Psi_{{\mathfrak{X}}_{\delta_{o},m,{\mathbb{C}}_{o}}}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\right)\tr\left((\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}}\times\tau)_{y}|({\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,\overline{\kappa}})_{z}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y}\tr(\tau\times h|[R\psi_{\delta_{o}}])\tr\left((\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}}\times\tau)_{y}|({\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,\overline{\kappa}})_{z}\right)=\sum_{y}\phi_{\tau,h}(\delta_{o})\tr\left((\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}}\times\tau)_{y}|({\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,\overline{\kappa}})_{z}\right),

where
[R\psi_{\delta_{o}}]
is the virtual
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})\times I_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
-admissible/continuous representation of
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o})\times W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
defined in
1.15
, and
\phi_{\tau,h}
in
C_{c}^{\infty}(\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o,r}))
is the function defined in
1.16
.

7.3.

In order to express the above sum in terms of orbital integrals, we now introduce
Langlands–Kottwitz counting
. This technique counts isomorphism classes of
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves over
\overline{\kappa}
whose zero lies over
o
by

(1)

first describing the
isogeny classes
of
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves in terms of conjugacy classes in certain groups,

(2)

then counting the number of isomorphism classes in each isogeny class by taking orbital integrals.

Since
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves consist of vector bundles, it is quite natural to reinterpret them in terms of linear algebraic groups. This incarnation of the Langlands–Kottwitz method is heavily based on work of Drinfeld and Laumon.

7.4.

To define isogeny classes of
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves, we first introduce
\varphi
-spaces
.

Definition
.

A
\varphi
-space
is a pair
(V,\varphi)
, where

\bullet

V
is a finite-dimensional
\mathbf{F}\otimes_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}
-vector space,

\bullet

\varphi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{}^{\sigma}\!V\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}V
is an
\mathbf{F}\otimes_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}
-linear isomorphism,

where
σ
denotes
(\id_{\mathbf{F}}\otimes_{\kappa}\sigma)^{*}
.

Note that
\varphi
-spaces resemble local shtukas and
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves, with an important difference:
\varphi
-spaces lie over the generic fiber of the curve
C
, rather than completions of
C
at closed points in the case of local shtukas or (a specific open subset of) the entire curve
C
in the case of
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves.

7.5.

For any
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
over
\overline{\kappa}
, the generic fiber
{\mathscr{E}}_{1,\mathbf{F}\otimes_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}}
is a finite-dimensional
\mathbf{F}\otimes_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}
-vector space, and the map
(j_{1,\mathbf{F}\otimes_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}})^{-1}\circ t_{1,\mathbf{F}\otimes_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}}
is a
\mathbf{F}\otimes_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}
-linear isomorphism. Therefore the pair

\displaystyle(V,\varphi)\coloneqq\left({\mathscr{E}}_{1,\mathbf{F}\otimes_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}},(j_{1,\mathbf{F}\otimes_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}})^{-1}\circ t_{1,\mathbf{F}\otimes_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}}\right)

forms a
\varphi
-space, and it has a right action of
D
for which
V
is a free right
D
-module of rank
1
. We call this the
generic fiber
of
