({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},f_{i})_{i}
, and we say two
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves over
\overline{\kappa}
are
isogenous
if they have isomorphic generic fibers. Thus isogeny classes of
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves correspond to certain isomorphism classes of
\varphi
-spaces.

Suppose that the zero of
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
lies over
o
. For every place
x
of
\mathbf{F}
, fix a map
\kappa_{x}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\overline{\kappa}
over
\kappa
, and write
{\mathscr{M}}_{x}
for the resulting local shtuka of
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
at
x
as constructed in
5.16
for
x=o
or
5.22
for
x\neq o
. Since the set of all maps
\kappa_{x}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\overline{\kappa}
over
\kappa
are cyclically permuted via composition with
\sigma
, we can view
{\mathscr{M}}_{x}
as a free module over

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{x}\widehat{\otimes}_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}=\prod_{\iota}{\mathcal{O}}_{x}\widehat{\otimes}_{\kappa_{x},\iota}\overline{\kappa}

equipped with a
\sigma
-semilinear automorphism after inverting
\varpi_{x}
, where the
\iota
runs over all maps
\kappa_{x}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\overline{\kappa}
over
\kappa

[
17
, p. 33]
. Under this perspective, we may naturally identify
({\mathscr{M}}_{x}[\frac{1}{\varpi_{x}}],{\mathscr{F}}_{x})
with
(V\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{F}_{x},\varphi\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{F}}\id_{\mathbf{F}_{x}})
.

7.6.

Next, we introduce
\varphi
-pairs
, which will provide an alternative way of describing
\varphi
-spaces.

Definition
.

A
\varphi
-pair
is a pair
(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})
, where

\bullet

\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}
is a finite separable (ring) extension of
\mathbf{F}
,

\bullet

\widetilde{\Pi}
is an element of
\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}^{\times}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
that is not contained in
\mathbf{F}^{\prime\times}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathbb{Q}}
for any proper
\mathbf{F}
-subalgebra
\mathbf{F}^{\prime}
of
\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}
.

Note that
\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}
is a product of field extensions of
\mathbf{F}
, so it has a well-defined notion of places. For any place
\widetilde{x}
of
\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}
, the
{\mathbb{Q}}
-valued valuation
\widetilde{x}(\widetilde{\Pi})
is well-defined, and we write
d(\widetilde{\Pi})
for the least common denominator of
\widetilde{x}(\widetilde{\Pi})\deg\widetilde{x}
as
\widetilde{x}
ranges over all places of
\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}
, where
\deg\widetilde{x}
is taken with respect to
\kappa
.

Therefore
\varphi
-pairs roughly correspond to elements in extensions (up to roots of unity) that cannot be obtained from subextensions by taking rational powers.

7.7.

The notions of a
\varphi
-pair and a
\varphi
-space are related as follows.

Proposition

(
[
17
, Proposition 2.1]
)
.

We have a canonical map

\displaystyle\{\mbox{isomorphism classes of }\varphi\mbox{-spaces}\}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\{\mbox{isomorphism classes of }\varphi\mbox{-pairs}\}

\displaystyle(V,\varphi)

\displaystyle\longmapsto(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{(V,\varphi)},\widetilde{\Pi}_{(V,\varphi)})

that induces a bijection from isomorphism classes of irreducible
\varphi
-spaces to isomorphism classes of
\varphi
-pairs for which
\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}
is a field. In that situation, the dimension of
V
over
\mathbf{F}\otimes_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}
equals
[\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{(V,\varphi)}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}]d(\widetilde{\Pi}_{(V,\varphi)})
, and the endomorphism ring of
(V,\varphi)
is isomorphic to the central division algebra
\widetilde{D}
over
\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{(V,\varphi)}
with Hasse invariants

\displaystyle\inv_{\widetilde{x}}(\widetilde{D})=-\widetilde{x}(\widetilde{\Pi}_{(V,\varphi)})\deg\widetilde{x}.

The above map is constructed by replacing
\overline{\kappa}
with a finite extension of
\kappa
of degree
a
and then considering the
\mathbf{F}
-algebra generated by
\varphi^{a}
, where taking
a
to be sufficiently divisible ensures that the resulting construction is independent of the choice of
a

[
17
, p. 31]
. This is the equi-characteristic analogue of Honda–Tate theory.

7.8.

We can describe which
\varphi
-spaces occur as generic fibers of
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves in terms of their associated
\varphi
-pairs. This completes our description of the isogeny classes of
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves.

Proposition

(
[
35
, (9.13)]
)
.

The isomorphism classes of
\varphi
-spaces that arise from
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves over
\overline{\kappa}
with zero lying over
o
map precisely to
\varphi
-pairs
(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})
for which

\bullet

\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}
is a field, and
[\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}]
divides
n
,

\bullet

there exists only one place
\widetilde{\infty}
of
\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}
lying over
\infty
, and it satisfies
\widetilde{\infty}(\widetilde{\Pi})\deg\widetilde{\infty}=-[\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}]/n
,

\bullet

there exists only one other place
\widetilde{o}\neq\widetilde{\infty}
of
\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}
satisfying
\widetilde{o}(\widetilde{\Pi})\neq 0
, and it lies over
o
.

For any
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
lying in the isogeny class corresponding to
(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})
, the height of the connected part of
{\mathscr{M}}_{o}^{\prime}
equals
n[\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{\widetilde{o}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}_{o}]/[\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}]
. Furthermore, if we write
(W,\psi)
for the irreducible
\varphi
-space corresponding to
(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})
, then
(V,\varphi)
is isomorphic to
(W,\psi)^{\oplus n}
.

By checking Hasse invariants of the endomorphism ring
\widetilde{D}
of
(W,\psi)
and applying Proposition
7.7
, we also deduce that
\widetilde{o}(\widetilde{\Pi})\deg\widetilde{o}=[\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}]/n
.

Return to the proof of Proposition
7.1
. By gathering terms in the same isogeny class, we can rewrite our sum as

\displaystyle\sum_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})}\sum_{y}\phi_{\tau,h}(\delta_{o})\tr\left((\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}}\times\tau)_{y}|({\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,\overline{\kappa}})_{z}\right),

where
(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})
runs over all
\varphi
-pairs satisfying the conditions in Proposition
7.8
, and
y
runs over
\overline{\kappa}
-points of
\Fix(\sigma^{r}_{o}\circ c_{\overline{\kappa}})
that lie in the isogeny class corresponding to
(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})
.

7.9.

At this point, we initiate the second part of the Langlands–Kottwitz method: counting the number of isomorphism classes in each isogeny class. We begin by describing
all
isomorphism classes—we shall refine our description by isogeny class afterwards.

Proposition

(
[
35
, (9.4)]
)
.

The map
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}\mapsto((V,\varphi),({\mathscr{M}}_{x})_{x})
, where

\bullet

(V,\varphi)
is the generic fiber of
({\mathscr{E}}_{i},t_{i},j_{i})_{i}
,

\bullet

x
runs through all places of
\mathbf{F}
, and
{\mathscr{M}}_{x}
is as in
7.5
,

yields a bijection from isomorphism classes of
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves over
\overline{\kappa}
with zero lying over
o
to isomorphism classes of pairs
((V,\varphi),({\mathscr{M}}_{x})_{x})
, where

\bullet

(V,\varphi)
is a
\varphi
-space with a right action of
D
such that
V
is a free right
D\otimes_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}
-module of rank
1
,

\bullet

x
runs over all places in
\mathbf{F}
, and
{\mathscr{M}}_{x}
is a local shtuka over
\overline{\kappa}
corresponding to a right
{\mathscr{D}}_{x}\widehat{\otimes}_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}
-submodule of rank
1
in
V\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{F}_{x}
that is stable under
\varphi\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{F}_{x}
,

that satisfy the following conditions:

(i)

the
\varphi
-pair corresponding to
(V,\varphi)
satisfies the conditions in Proposition
7.8
,

(ii)

the Morita reduction
{\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{o}
of
{\mathscr{M}}_{o}
is effective minuscule of dimension
1
and rank
n
,

(iii)

the isogeny class of the Morita reduction
{\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{\infty}
of
{\mathscr{M}}_{\infty}
has slope
-\frac{1}{n}
under the Dieudonné–Manin classification,

(iv)

{\mathscr{M}}_{x}
is étale for all closed points
x
in
C\smallsetminus\{o,\infty\}
,

(v)

there exists a generator
b
of
V
as a free right
D\otimes_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}
-module such that, for cofinitely many
x
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathscr{M}}_{x}=b({\mathscr{D}}_{x}\widehat{\otimes}_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa})

as right
{\mathscr{D}}_{x}
-modules equipped with a
\sigma
-semilinear automorphism, where we view
{\mathscr{M}}_{x}
as the corresponding free
{\mathscr{D}}_{x}\widehat{\otimes}_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}
-submodule of
V\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{F}_{x}
of rank
1
equipped with a
\sigma
-semilinear automorphism.

7.10.

Next, we use Proposition
7.9
to obtain a description of the isomorphism classes in a given isogeny class. Fix a
\varphi
-pair
(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})
satisfying the conditions of Proposition
7.8
, and write
\widetilde{D}
for the endomorphism ring of
(V_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})},\varphi_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})})
. Write
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}(\overline{\kappa})_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})}
for the set of points in
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}(\overline{\kappa})
lying in the isogeny class corresponding to
(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})
. Our goal is to describe
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}(\overline{\kappa})_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})}
using algebraic group-theoretic data.

Let
k=n[\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{\widetilde{o}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}_{o}]/[\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}]
, write
\varepsilon_{o}^{\widetilde{o}}
for the identity matrix in
\GL_{n-k}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
, write
\varepsilon_{\widetilde{o}}
for the
k
-by-
k
matrix

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\widetilde{o}}\coloneqq\begin{bmatrix}0&1&&\\
&\ddots&\ddots&\\
&&\ddots&1\\
\varpi_{o}&&&0\end{bmatrix}\in\GL_{k}(\mathbf{F}_{o}),

and write
\varepsilon_{o}
for the block matrix
\varepsilon_{o}^{\widetilde{o}}\oplus\varepsilon_{\widetilde{o}}
in
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
. Note that, for any
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaf in
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}(\overline{\kappa})_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})}
, Proposition
7.9
says that its local shtuka at
o
corresponds to
\varepsilon_{o}
via
1.2
, by the Dieudonné–Manin classification.

Write
{\mathscr{Y}}_{o}
for the subset

\displaystyle{\mathscr{Y}}_{o}\coloneqq\{h_{o}\in\GL_{n}(\breve{\mathbf{F}}_{o})|h_{o}^{-1}\varepsilon_{o}\sigma_{o}(h_{o})\in\langle\varpi_{o}\rangle\},

and write
\breve{\mathscr{K}}_{o,m}
for the subgroup

\displaystyle\breve{\mathscr{K}}_{o,m}\coloneqq\ker\left(\GL_{n}(\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o})\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\GL_{n}(\breve{\mathcal{O}}_{o}/\varpi_{o}^{m})\right).

Proposition
.

We have a bijection

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{I}(\overline{\kappa})_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}\widetilde{D}^{\times}\backslash\left((D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty,o})^{\times}/{\mathscr{K}}^{\infty,o}_{I}\times{\mathscr{Y}}_{o}/\breve{\mathscr{K}}_{o,m}\right)

for which
g^{\infty,o}
acts on the right-hand side via left multiplication on
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty,o})^{\times}
, and
\sigma_{o}^{-1}
acts on the right-hand side via sending
h_{o}\mapsto\varepsilon_{o}\sigma_{o}(h)
on
{\mathscr{Y}}_{o}
.

Proof.

We use the description of
{\mathcal{M}}_{I}(\overline{\kappa})_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})}
given in Proposition
7.9
. Under this description, the translation action of
{\mathbb{Z}}
sends
((V,\varphi),({\mathscr{M}}_{x})_{x})
to
((V,\varphi),(\varphi^{l}({\mathscr{M}}_{x}))_{x})
for all integers
l
. The characterization of
{\mathscr{M}}_{\infty}^{\prime}
given in Proposition
7.9
.(iii) implies that we may fix the position of
{\mathscr{M}}^{\prime}_{\infty}
in
V\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{F}_{\infty}
via translating by
{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Next, fix a generator
b
of
V
as a free right
D\otimes_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}
-module. For all closed points
x
in
C\smallsetminus\infty
, let
h_{x}
be an element of
D_{x}
such that
bh_{x}
generates
{\mathscr{M}}_{x}
over
{\mathscr{D}}_{x}\widehat{\otimes}_{\kappa}\overline{\kappa}
. Parts (iv) and (v) of Proposition
7.9
show that
(h_{x})_{x}
is an element of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty,o})^{\times}
as
x
runs over all closed points in
C\smallsetminus\{o,\infty\}
, and the level-
I
structures shows that
(h_{x})_{x}
is well-defined up to right multiplication by
{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty,o}
. Similarly,
h_{o}
yields a well-defined element of
{\mathscr{Y}}_{o}/\breve{\mathscr{K}}_{o,m}
.

Finally, the left action of
\widetilde{D}^{\times}
on
(V,\varphi)
yields an embedding

\displaystyle\widetilde{D}^{\times}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow}}(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty,o})^{\times}\times{\mathscr{Y}}_{o}

that preserves the action of
\widetilde{D}^{\times}
on
(V,\varphi)
, and taking the quotient of

\displaystyle(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty,o})^{\times}/{\mathscr{K}}^{\infty,o}_{I}\times{\mathscr{Y}}_{o}/\breve{\mathscr{K}}_{o,m}

with respect to this action concludes the proof, by Proposition
7.9
.
∎

7.11.

With Proposition
7.10
in hand, we shall give an analogous description of
\Fix(\sigma_{o}^{r}\circ\pi_{*}c_{\overline{\kappa}})
. Write
\Fix(\sigma_{o}^{r}\circ\pi_{*}c_{\overline{\kappa}})_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})}
for the set of
y
in
\Fix(\sigma_{o}^{r}\circ\pi_{*}c_{\overline{\kappa}})
lying in the isogeny class corresponding to
(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})
. Our goal is to describe
\Fix(\sigma_{o}^{r}\circ\pi_{*}c_{\overline{\kappa}})_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})}
in terms of the bijection from Proposition
7.10
.

Write
{\mathscr{K}}_{r,m}
for the subgroup

\displaystyle{\mathscr{K}}_{r,m}\coloneqq\ker\left(\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o,r})\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o,r}/\varpi_{o}^{m})\right).

For any element
\widetilde{d}
in
\widetilde{D}^{\times}
, we say
\widetilde{d}
is
r
-admissible
if there exists some
h_{\widetilde{d}}
in
\GL_{n}(\breve{\mathbf{F}}_{o})
such that

\displaystyle h^{-1}_{\widetilde{d}}\cdot\widetilde{d}^{-1}\cdot\mathrm{N}_{o}\varepsilon_{o}\cdot\sigma_{o}^{r}(h_{\widetilde{d}})=1,

where we view
\widetilde{d}^{-1}
as an element of
(\widetilde{D}\otimes_{\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}}\breve{\mathbf{F}}_{o})^{\times}=\GL_{n}(\breve{\mathbf{F}}_{o})
, and
\mathrm{N}_{o}
denotes the norm map. Note that
r
-admissibility descends to a property on
\widetilde{D}^{\times}
-conjugacy classes in
\widetilde{D}^{\times}
.

For any group
G
and element
g
in
G
, we denote the centralizer of
g
in
G
by
G_{g}
. We write
{\mathscr{Y}}_{\widetilde{d}}
for the double quotient space

\displaystyle{\mathscr{Y}}_{\widetilde{d}}\coloneqq\widetilde{D}^{\times}_{\widetilde{d}}\backslash\left((D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty,o})^{\times}/{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty,o}\times\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o,r})/{\mathscr{K}}_{r,m}\right).

Proposition
.

We have a bijection from
\Fix(\sigma_{o}^{r}\circ\pi_{*}c_{\overline{\kappa}})_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})}
to the disjoint union

\displaystyle\coprod_{\widetilde{d}}\left\{\widetilde{D}^{\times}_{\widetilde{d}}\left(h^{\infty,o}{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty,o},h_{\widetilde{d}}h_{r}{\mathscr{K}}_{r,m}\right)\in{\mathscr{Y}}_{\widetilde{d}}\middle|(h^{\infty,o})^{-1}\widetilde{d}h^{\infty,o}\in{\mathscr{K}}^{\infty,o}_{I}g^{o}{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty,o}\mbox{ and }h_{r}^{-1}\widetilde{\gamma}\sigma_{o}(h_{r})\in\langle\varpi_{o}\rangle\cap\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o,r})\right\},

where
\widetilde{d}
runs over all
r
-admissible
\widetilde{D}^{\times}
-conjugacy classes in
\widetilde{D}^{\times}
, and
\widetilde{\gamma}
is defined to be
h_{\widetilde{d}}^{-1}\varepsilon_{o}\sigma_{o}^{r}(h_{\widetilde{d}})
.

Note that
\widetilde{\gamma}
is fixed by
\sigma_{o}^{r}
and hence lies in
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o,r})
.

Proof of Proposition
Proposition
.

Begin by applying Proposition
7.9
to
g^{o,-1}{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty,o}g^{o}
instead of
{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty,o}
to obtain an adelic description of
\Fix(\sigma_{o}^{r}\circ\pi_{*}c_{\overline{\kappa}})_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})}
as in
[
34
, p. 53]
. Next, simplify the resulting description by using our largeness hypothesis on
I
: this ensures that
{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty,o}
is small enough to apply the same argument as in
[
34
, (3.2.6)]
. Conclude as in
[
34
, (3.2.7)]
.
∎

Return to the proof of Proposition
7.1
. Since
\delta_{o}
corresponds to
h_{r}^{-1}\widetilde{\gamma}\sigma_{o}(h_{r})
under Proposition
7.11
, our sum equals

\displaystyle\sum_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})}\sum_{\widetilde{d}}\vol\left(\widetilde{D}^{\times}_{\widetilde{d}}\backslash\left((D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty,o})^{\times}_{\widetilde{d}}\times\GL_{n,\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\sigma_{o}}(\mathbf{F}_{o})\right)\right)\operatorname{O}_{\widetilde{d}}(f^{\infty,o})\TO_{\widetilde{\gamma},\sigma_{o}}(\phi_{\tau,h})\tr\left((\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}}\times\tau)_{y}|({\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,\overline{\kappa}})_{z}\right),

where
\widetilde{d}
runs over all
r
-admissible
\widetilde{D}^{\times}
-conjugacy classes in
\widetilde{D}^{\times}
, and
\GL_{n,\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\sigma_{o}}
denotes the
\sigma_{o}
-centralizer of
\widetilde{\gamma}
in
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o,r})
. As before, we do not explicate our Haar measures, but we choose them compatibly whenever possible. We may rewrite the volume factors in the above sum via the following lemma.

7.12 Lemma

(
[
35
, (11.7)]
)
.

The embeddings

\displaystyle(\widetilde{D}\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty,o})^{\times}_{\widetilde{d}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow}}(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty,o})^{\times}_{\widetilde{d}}\mbox{ and }(\widetilde{D}\otimes\mathbf{F}_{o})^{\times}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow}}\GL_{n,\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\sigma_{o}}(\mathbf{F}_{o})

induced by the left action of
\widetilde{D}^{\times}
as in the proof of Proposition
7.10
are isomorphisms.

Return to the proof of Proposition
7.1
. Lemma
7.12
indicates that we can modify our volume terms to make our expression of interest equal

\displaystyle\sum_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})}\sum_{\widetilde{d}}\vol\left(\widetilde{D}^{\times}_{\widetilde{d}}\backslash(\widetilde{D}\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}_{\widetilde{d}}\right)\operatorname{O}_{\widetilde{d}}(f^{\infty,o})\TO_{\widetilde{\gamma},\sigma_{o}}(\phi_{\tau,h})\tr\left((\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}}\times\tau)_{y}|({\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,\overline{\kappa}})_{z}\right).

At this point, we have two goals: to rewrite this as a sum over certain conjugacy classes of
D^{\times}
instead of
\widetilde{D}^{\times}
, and to rewrite the trace on
({\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi})_{z}
in terms of algebraic group-theoretic data. For this, we shall use the following lemma, which transfers both conjugacy classes as well as volumes of their stabilizers.

7.13.

Write
\rn\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}D\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{F}
for the reduced norm of
D
, and let
\gamma
be an element of
D^{\times}
. Write
\mathbf{F}^{\prime}
for the finite extension
\mathbf{F}[\gamma]
of
\mathbf{F}
. We say that
\gamma
is
r
-admissible
if
o(\rn\gamma)=r
and there exists a place
o^{\prime}
of
\mathbf{F}^{\prime}
above
o
such that, for all other places
x^{\prime}\neq o^{\prime}
of
\mathbf{F}^{\prime}
lying above
o
, we have
x^{\prime}(\gamma)=0
. Note that
r
-admissibility descends to a property on
D^{\times}
-conjugacy classes in
D^{\times}
.

Lemma

(
[
35
, (11.9)]
,
[
34
, (3.5.4)]
)
.

We have a bijection

\displaystyle\{D^{\times}\mbox{-conjugacy classes in }D^{\times}\mbox{ that are }r\mbox{-admissible and elliptic in }D_{\infty}^{\times}\}

\displaystyle\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}\coprod_{(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})}\{\widetilde{D}^{\times}\mbox{-conjugacy classes in }\widetilde{D}^{\times}\mbox{ that are }r\mbox{-admissible}\},

where
(\widetilde{\mathbf{F}},\widetilde{\Pi})
ranges over all
\varphi
-pairs satisfying the conditions of Proposition
7.8
. If we denote this bijection by
\gamma\mapsto\widetilde{d}
, then the
D^{\times}_{o}=\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
-conjugacy class of
\mathrm{N}_{o}\widetilde{\gamma}
equals that of
\gamma
, and we have an equality of traces

\displaystyle\tr\left((\pi_{\overline{\kappa},*}c_{\overline{\kappa}}\times\tau)_{y}|({\mathcal{L}}_{I\smallsetminus o,\xi,\overline{\kappa}})_{z}\right)=\Theta_{\xi}(\overline{\gamma}),

where
\overline{\gamma}
is any elliptic element of
\overline{B}^{\times}
with the same characteristic polynomial as
\gamma
, and
\Theta_{\xi}
is the character of
\xi
. Furthermore, we have an equality of volumes

\displaystyle\vol\left(\widetilde{D}^{\times}_{\widetilde{d}}\backslash(\widetilde{D}\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}_{\widetilde{d}}\right)=a(\gamma)\frac{1}{n\cdot\vol(\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\widetilde{D}_{\widetilde{\infty}}^{\times})},

where
a(\gamma)
is another volume factor

\displaystyle a(\gamma)\coloneqq\vol\left(\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}D^{\times}_{\gamma}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}_{\gamma}\right).

Return to the proof of Proposition
7.1
. The above lemma implies that our sum becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma}a(\gamma)\frac{\varepsilon(\gamma)}{n\cdot\vol(\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\widetilde{D}_{\widetilde{\infty}}^{\times})}\operatorname{O}_{\gamma}(f^{\infty,o})\varepsilon(\gamma)\TO_{\widetilde{\gamma},\sigma_{o}}(\phi_{\tau,h})\Theta_{\xi}(\overline{\gamma}),

where
\gamma
runs over all
D^{\times}
-conjugacy classes in
D^{\times}
that are
r
-admissible and elliptic in
D^{\times}_{\infty}
, and
\varepsilon(\gamma)
is the Kottwitz sign
\varepsilon(\gamma)\coloneqq(-1)^{n/[\mathbf{F}_{\infty}[\gamma]\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}_{\infty}]-1}
. Since
\phi_{\tau,h}
and
f_{\tau,h}
have matching orbital integrals, applying Lemma
7.13
further changes our sum to

\displaystyle\sum_{\gamma}a(\gamma)\frac{\varepsilon(\gamma)}{n\cdot\vol(\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\widetilde{D}_{\widetilde{\infty}}^{\times})}\operatorname{O}_{\gamma}(f^{\infty,o})\operatorname{O}_{\gamma}(f_{\tau,h})\Theta_{\xi}(\overline{\gamma})=\sum_{\gamma}a(\gamma)\frac{\varepsilon(\gamma)}{n\cdot\vol(\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\widetilde{D}_{\widetilde{\infty}}^{\times})}\Theta_{\xi}(\overline{\gamma})\operatorname{O}_{\gamma}(f^{\infty,o}\times f_{\tau,h}).

We now want to absorb the
\varepsilon(\gamma)\vol(\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\widetilde{D}_{\widetilde{\infty}}^{\times})^{-1}\Theta_{\xi}(\overline{\gamma})
term into our orbital integral. In order to do so, we shall introduce the following special function on
D^{\times}_{\infty}=\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty})
.

7.14.

We now introduce
pseudo-coefficients
, which are certain functions in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty}))
. Recall that
\varpi_{\infty}
acts trivially under
\xi
, which implies that
\xi
has unitary central character. Therefore the local Jacquet–Langlands correspondence as in
4.8
yields an irreducible
L^{2}
representation
\JL^{-1}(\xi)
of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty})
. Let
f_{\xi,\infty}
in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty}))
be the corresponding pseudo-coefficient of
\JL^{-1}(\xi)
as in
[
6
, Section 5]
.

Lemma

(
[
6
, p. 2203]
,
[
35
, (13.8)]
)
.

Let
\gamma
be an element of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty})
. Then the function
f_{\xi,\infty}
satisfies

\displaystyle\operatorname{O}_{\gamma}(f_{\xi,\infty})=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon(\gamma)}{\vol(\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash\widetilde{D}_{\widetilde{\infty}}^{\times})}\Theta_{\xi}(\overline{\gamma})&\mbox{if }\gamma\mbox{ is elliptic,}\\
0&\mbox{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Return to the proof of Proposition
7.1
. The pseudo-coefficient
f_{\xi,\infty}
allows us to rewrite our sum as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\gamma}a(\gamma)\operatorname{O}_{\gamma}(f_{\xi,\infty}\times f^{\infty,o}\times f_{\tau,h}).

7.15.

Before using the Selberg trace formula, we first need some notation regarding automorphic representations. Write
{\mathcal{A}}(\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}D^{\times}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times})
for the
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
-vector space

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}(\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}D^{\times}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times})\coloneqq\{f\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}D^{\times}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\mid f\mbox{ is locally constant}\}.

Then
{\mathcal{A}}(\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}D^{\times}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times})
has a left action of
\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}
via right multiplication, and we see that this is a smooth representation of
\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}
. Now
\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}D^{\times}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}
is compact because
D
is a division algebra, so we obtain a decomposition

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}(\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}D^{\times}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times})=\bigoplus_{\widetilde{\Pi}}\widetilde{\Pi}^{\oplus m(\widetilde{\Pi})},

where
\widetilde{\Pi}
ranges over all irreducible admissible representations of
\varpi^{\mathbb{Z}}_{\infty}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}
, and
m(\widetilde{\Pi})
is a non-negative integer. If
m(\widetilde{\Pi})
is nonzero, we say
\widetilde{\Pi}
is
automorphic
. The weak multiplicity one theorem
[
6
, Theorem 3.3.(a)]
indicates that
m(\widetilde{\Pi})
is at most
1
.

Return to the proof of Proposition
7.1
. Applying the Selberg trace formula to
{\mathcal{A}}(\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}D^{\times}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times})
shows that our sum of orbital integrals equals the sum of traces

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\widetilde{\Pi}}m(\widetilde{\Pi})\tr(f_{\xi,\infty}\times f^{\infty,o}\times f_{\tau,h}|\widetilde{\Pi}),

where
\widetilde{\Pi}
runs over all irreducible automorphic representations of
\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}
. Therefore the following proposition of Laumon–Rapoport–Stuhler concludes the proof of Proposition
7.1
.
∎

7.16 Proposition
.

We have an equality of traces

\displaystyle\sum_{\widetilde{\Pi}}m(\widetilde{\Pi})\tr(f_{\xi,\infty}\times f^{\infty,o}\times f_{\tau,h}|\widetilde{\Pi})=\tr(f^{\infty,o}\times f_{\tau,h}|[H_{\xi}]),

where
\widetilde{\Pi}
ranges over all irreducible automorphic representations of
\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}
.

Proof.

This is the generalization of
[
35
, (13.6)]
as described in
[
35
, (13.8)]
. This generalization relies on the existence of the pseudo-coefficient
f_{\xi,\infty}
, which we have already seen exists as in
[
6
, Section 5]
.
∎

We remark that the proof of this proposition uses similar point-counting methods as we do, except that Laumon–Rapoport–Stuhler immediately pass to the level of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}
, rather than remaining at the level of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o,r})
at the place
o
. The latter is necessary for incorporating the contributions of
\phi_{\tau,h}
and hence
f_{\tau,h}
.

8.
Local-global compatibility

Our goal in this section is to prove that condition (c) in Lemma
2.7
holds, which completes our proof of Theorem A. Along the way, we shall prove Theorem B. Returning to the local notation of §
1
–§
4
for a moment, we shall construct the desired virtual representation
\rho(\pi)
of
W_{F}
from our cohomology representation
[H_{\xi}]
.

Now revert back to global notation conventions. We start by recalling results of Laumon–Rapoport–Stuhler that specify the automorphic representations of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}
occurring in
[H_{\xi}]
. Next, we use the
strong multiplicity one theorem
, which says that automorphic representations of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}
are determined by their local components at cofinitely many places, to convert Proposition
7.1
into a global analog of condition (c) in Lemma
2.7
. Finally, we introduce the
global Jacquet–Langlands correspondence
, which enables us to pass between automorphic representations of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}
and
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
. This finishes the proof of Theorem B, our titular local-global compatibility result.

At this point, we want to use Theorem B to prove condition (c) in Lemma
2.7
. To do so, we need to embed local representations of
\GL_{n}
into global representations. We start by presenting such an embedding for
L^{2}
representations, which uses the
Deligne–Kazhdan simple trace formula
. Trace formula methods also allow us to embed cuspidal representations globally. From here, we use Mœglin–Waldspurger’s description of the discrete automorphic spectrum of
\GL_{n}
in order to realize Speh modules in a global setting, and this enables us to complete the proof of condition (c) in Lemma
2.7
.

8.1.

First, we introduce some notation on multiplicity spaces. Let
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}
be an irreducible admissible representation of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}
, and write
H_{\xi,\eta}^{i}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})
for the
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
-vector space

\displaystyle H^{i}_{\xi,\eta}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})\coloneqq\Hom_{(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty},H^{i,\text{ss}}_{\xi,\eta}),

that is, the multiplicity of
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}
in
H^{i,\text{ss}}_{\xi,\eta}
, where
H^{i,\text{ss}}_{\xi,\eta}
denotes the semisimplification of
H^{i}_{\xi,\eta}
. We see that
H^{i}_{\xi,\eta}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})
is a continuous finite-dimensional representation of
G_{\mathbf{F}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
, where
G_{\mathbf{F}}
denotes the Galois group of the global field
\mathbf{F}
. Write
[H_{\xi}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})]
for the virtual representation
\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{i}H^{i}_{\xi,\eta}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})
, which we see equals the multiplicity of
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}
in
[H_{\xi}]
. Therefore
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}
occurs in
[H_{\xi}]
if and only if
[H_{\xi}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})]
is nonzero.

8.2.

The question of whether
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}
occurs in
[H_{\xi}]
is connected to automorphic representations of
\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}
as well as the representation
\xi
of
\overline{B}^{\times}
. Since
\varpi_{\infty}
acts trivially under
\xi
, we see
\xi
has unitary central character. Therefore the representation
\JL^{-1}(\xi)
as in
4.8
is an irreducible
L^{2}
representation of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty})
and hence isomorphic to a Steinberg module

\displaystyle\JL^{-1}(\xi)=\St_{t}(\pi_{0,\xi})

as in
2.3
, where
t
is a positive divisor of
n
,
d=\frac{n}{t}
, and
\pi_{0,\xi}
is an irreducible cuspidal representation of
\GL_{d}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty})
with unitary central character. Recall that we can also form the Speh module
\Sp_{t}(\pi_{0,\xi})
as in
2.3
, which is an irreducible smooth representation of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty})
The following result of Laumon–Rapoport–Stuhler determines precisely when
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}
occurs in
[H_{\xi}]
.

Proposition
.

(i)

The virtual representation
[H_{\xi}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})]
of
G_{\mathbf{F}}
is nonzero if and only if either
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}\otimes\St_{t}(\pi_{0,\xi})
or
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}\otimes\Sp_{t}(\pi_{0,\xi})
is an irreducible automorphic representation of
\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}
. Furthermore, we have
\dim[H_{\xi}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})]=n
.

(ii)

In addition, we have
H^{i}_{\xi,\eta}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})=0
either for all odd
i
or all even
i
.

Proof.

Part (ii) and the first statement of part (i) comprise the generalization of
[
35
, (14.7)]
as described in
[
35
, (14.21)]
. This generalization follows from the global Jacquet–Langlands correspondence between
D^{\times}
and
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F})
(which is now known
[
6
, Theorem 3.2]
), by the remark made in
[
35
, (14.24)]
. As for the dimension statement in part (i), this follows from Deligne’s purity theorem, c.f.
[
35
, (14.11)(ii)]
.
∎

8.3.

Next, we use Proposition
7.1
to obtain the following result, which resembles condition (c) in Lemma
2.7
.

Proposition
.

Let
\tau
be an element of
W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
with
v_{o}(\tau)>0
, and let
h
be a function in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{o}))
. Let
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}
be an irreducible admissible representation of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}
for which
[H_{\xi}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})]
is nonzero. Then we have an equality of traces

\displaystyle\tr(f_{\tau,h}|\widetilde{\Pi}_{o}^{\infty})=\tr(\tau|[H_{\xi}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})])\tr(h|\widetilde{\Pi}_{o}^{\infty}),

where
\widetilde{\Pi}_{o}^{\infty}
denotes the component of
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}
at
o
.

For any irreducible admissible representation
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\prime\infty}
of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}
, we write
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\prime\infty,o}
for the component of
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\prime\infty}
away from
o
.

Proof.

Let
I
be a sufficiently large finite closed subscheme of
C\smallsetminus\infty
such that
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty,o}
has nonzero
{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty,o}
-invariants and
h
is invariant under
{\mathscr{K}}_{I,o}^{\infty}
. Because
[H_{\xi}]
is admissible, we see that
[H_{\xi}]^{{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty}}
is finite-dimensional, so
[H_{\xi}]
can only contain finitely many non-isomorphic irreducible admissible representations
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\prime\infty}
of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty})^{\times}
with nonzero
{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty}
-invariants. By applying the Chinese remainder theorem to these left
C^{\infty}_{c}((D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty,o})^{\times}\!/\!/{\mathscr{K}}_{I}^{\infty,o})
-modules
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\prime\infty}
, we obtain a function
f^{\infty,o}
in
C^{\infty}_{c}((D\otimes{\mathbb{A}}^{\infty,o})^{\times})
satisfying the following properties:

(i)

\tr(f^{\infty,o}|\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty,o})=1
,

(ii)

if
\tr(f^{\infty,o}|\widetilde{\Pi}^{\prime\infty})\neq 0
for one of our aforementioned
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\prime\infty}
, then
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\prime\infty,o}
is isomorphic to
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty,o}
.

In order to strengthen property (ii) to show that
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\prime\infty}
is isomorphic to
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}
(hence extending our isomorphism to the component at
o
), we use the
strong multiplicity one theorem
. We recall its statement below.

8.4.

Let
T
be a finite set of places of
\mathbf{F}
. For any irreducible admissible representation
\widetilde{\Pi}
of
\varpi^{\mathbb{Z}}_{\infty}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}
, we write
\widetilde{\Pi}^{T}
for the component of
\widetilde{\Pi}
away from
T
, and we write
\widetilde{\Pi}_{T}
for the component of
\widetilde{\Pi}
at
T
.

Theorem

(
[
6
, Theorem 3.3.(b)]
)
.

Let
\widetilde{\Pi}
and
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\prime}
be two irreducible automorphic representations of
\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}\backslash(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}
, and let
T
be a finite set of places of
\mathbf{F}
. If
\widetilde{\Pi}^{T}
is isomorphic to
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\prime T}
, then
\widetilde{\Pi}
is isomorphic to
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\prime}
.

We remark that
[
6
]
deduces the strong multiplicity one theorem by using the global Jacquet–Langlands correspondence
[
6
, Theorem 3.2]
to reduce to the case of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F})
.

Return to the proof of Proposition
8.3
. The strong multiplicity one theorem and Proposition
8.2
.(i) imply that, if the hypothesis of property (ii) holds, then
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\prime\infty}
is actually isomorphic to
\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty}
. Therefore plugging
f^{\infty,o}
into Proposition
7.1
shows that

\displaystyle\tr(h|\widetilde{\Pi}_{o}^{\infty})\tr(\tau|[H_{\xi}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})])=\tr(f^{\infty,o}\times h\times\tau|[H_{\xi}])=\textstyle\frac{1}{n}\tr(f^{\infty,o}\times f_{\tau,h}|[H_{\xi}])=\frac{1}{n}\dim[H_{\xi}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})]\tr(f_{\tau,h}|\widetilde{\Pi}_{o}^{\infty}),

and applying the second statement in Proposition
8.2
.(i) concludes the proof of Proposition
8.3
.
∎

8.5.

To convert automorphic representations of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
into those of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}
, we use the
global Jacquet–Langlands correspondence
. Recall from
5.2
that
Bad
denotes the set of places where
D
ramifies.

From this point onwards, assume that
D_{x}
is a division algebra for all places
x
in
Bad
. We say that an irreducible discrete automorphic representation
\Pi
of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
is
D
-admissible
if, for all places
x
in
Bad
, the local component
\Pi_{x}
is a Steinberg module or Speh module, as a representation of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x})
.

There exists a unique bijection
[
6
, Theorem 3.2]

\displaystyle\JL\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\left\{\begin{tabular}[]{c}isomorphism classes of irreducible\\
$D$-admissible representations of $\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})$\end{tabular}\right\}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}\left\{\begin{tabular}[]{c}isomorphism classes of irreducible\\
automorphic representations of $(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}$\end{tabular}\right\}

such that, for all places
x
of
\mathbf{F}
,

(i)

if
x
does not lie in
Bad
, then
\JL(\Pi)_{x}
is isomorphic to
\Pi_{x}
,

(ii)

if
x
lies in
Bad
, and
\Pi_{x}
is isomorphic to either
\St_{t}(\pi_{0})
or
\Sp_{t}(\pi_{0})
as in Definition
2.3
, then
\JL(\Pi)_{x}
is isomorphic to
\JL(\St_{t}(\pi_{0}))
, where the latter
\JL
denotes the local Jacquet–Langlands correspondence from
4.8
.

8.6.

Before using the global Jacquet–Langlands correspondence to deduce Theorem B, let us fix our choice of division algebra. Fix three distinct places
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
of
\mathbf{F}
, and let
D
be the central division algebra over
\mathbf{F}
of dimension
n^{2}
defined by

\displaystyle\inv_{x}(D)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{n}&\mbox{ if }x=x_{1},\\
-\frac{1}{n}&\mbox{ if }x=x_{2},\\
0&\mbox{otherwise,}\end{cases}

as in
5.2
. Let
{\mathscr{D}}
be a maximal order of
D
, which can be constructed using
5.4
because division algebras split at cofinitely many places.

8.7.

We now proceed to prove Theorem B, using Proposition
8.3
and the global Jacquet–Langlands correspondence.

Proposition
.

Let
\Pi
be an irreducible discrete automorphic representation of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
whose components at
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
are either irreducible
L^{2}
representations or Speh modules of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x_{1}})
,
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x_{2}})
, and
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty})
, respectively. Then there exists a unique
n
-dimensional semisimple continuous representation
R(\Pi)
of
G_{\mathbf{F}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
such that, for all places
o
of
\mathbf{F}
not lying in
\{x_{1},x_{2},\infty\}
, the restriction of
R(\Pi)
to
W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left.R(\Pi)\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}=\rho(\Pi_{o}),

where we identify
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
with
{\mathbb{C}}
.

Proof.

The Chebotarev density theorem implies that, as
o
varies over all places of
\mathbf{F}
not lying in
\{x_{1},x_{2},\infty\}
, the conjugacy classes of geometric
q_{o}
-Frobenius elements at
o
in
G_{\mathbf{F}}
are dense. Now
n
-dimensional continuous semisimple representations of
G_{\mathbf{F}}
are determined, up to isomorphism, by their characteristic polynomials, and said polynomials are continuous in
G_{\mathbf{F}}
, so we see that the above condition determines
R(\Pi)
uniquely.

We turn to the existence of
R(\Pi)
. Our hypotheses indicate that
\Pi
is
D
-admissible, so we can form the automorphic representation
\widetilde{\Pi}\coloneqq\JL(\Pi)
of
(D\otimes{\mathbb{A}})^{\times}
. Now
\widetilde{\Pi}_{\infty}=\Pi_{\infty}
is either a Steinberg module or a Speh module, so Proposition
8.2
.(i) ensures that there exists an irreducible smooth representation of
\overline{B}^{\times}/\varpi_{\infty}^{\mathbb{Z}}
such that
[H_{\xi}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})]
has dimension
n
. Finally, Proposition
8.3
indicates that
\left.[H_{\xi}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})]\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}
satisfies the defining condition Theorem A.(i) of
\rho(\Pi_{o})
, so taking
R(\Pi)=[H_{\xi}(\widetilde{\Pi}^{\infty})]
yields the desired result.
∎

8.8.

Briefly return to the local notation of §
1
–§
4
. In order to convert Theorem B into a proof of condition (c) in Lemma
2.7
, we must find some global automorphic representation
\widetilde{\Pi}
of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
such that our local representation
\pi
is isomorphic to
\widetilde{\Pi}_{o}
as a representation of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
. That is, we must embed local representations into global ones. Recall that the two classes of possibilities for
\pi
from condition (c) of Lemma
2.7
that we must consider are

(1)

essentially
L^{2}
representations, which we will reduce to the case of
L^{2}
representations via Proposition
3.5
,

(2)

Speh modules, which we reduce to the case of cuspidal representations by Mœglin–Waldspurger’s classification of the discrete automorphic spectrum of
\GL_{n}
.

8.9.

Return to our global notation. We start by embedding local
L^{2}
representations into global representations.

Lemma
.

Let
\pi
be an irreducible
L^{2}
representation of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
. Then there exists an irreducible automorphic representation
\Pi
of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
whose

(i)

component at
o
is isomorphic to
\pi
,

(ii)

components at
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
are irreducible
L^{2}
representations of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x_{1}})
,
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x_{2}})
, and
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty})
, respectively, with unitary central characters.

Proof.

This is proven precisely as in
[
20
, Corollary VI.2.5]
using the simple trace formula
[
15
, A.1.d Theorem]
.
∎

8.10.

As a first step towards embedding Speh modules into a global setting, we first realize certain local cuspidal representations in a global context.

Lemma
.

Let
\pi
be an irreducible cuspidal representation of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
with unitary central character. Then there exists an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation
\Pi
of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
whose

(i)

component at
o
is isomorphic to
\pi
,

(ii)

components at
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
are irreducible cuspidal representations of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x_{1}})
,
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x_{2}})
, and
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty})
, respectively, with unitary central characters.

Proof.

This follows from the proof of
[
35
, (15.2)]
, where we use
\infty
instead of
x_{3}
and
o
instead of
x_{0}
.
∎

8.11.

We want to use Mœglin–Waldspurger’s classification of the discrete automorphic spectrum of
\GL_{n}
. To present this classification, let us recall some notation from
2.2
on local representations. Briefly return to the local notation of §
1
–§
4
, and let
\{\Delta_{1},\dotsc,\Delta_{t}\}
be a collection of segments as in
[
49
, 3.1]
such that
\Delta_{i}
does not precede
\Delta_{j}
as in
[
49
, 4.1]
for
i<j
. Then each associated
Q(\Delta_{i})
is an irreducible essentially
L^{2}
representation of
\GL_{n_{i}}(F)
, and recall that
Q(\Delta_{1},\dotsc,\Delta_{t})
denotes the unique irreducible quotient of

\displaystyle\nInd_{P(F)}^{\GL_{n}(F)}\left(Q(\Delta_{1})\otimes\dotsb\otimes Q(\Delta_{t})\right),

where
n=n_{1}+\dotsb+n_{t}
, and
P
is the standard parabolic subgroup of
\GL_{n}
with block sizes
(n_{1},\dotsc,n_{t})
.

Now return to the global notation. Work of Mœglin–Waldspurger implies the following.

Lemma

(
[
38
, Theorem]
)
.

Let
d
be a positive divisor of
n
, write
d\coloneqq\frac{n}{t}
, and let
\Pi_{0}
be an irreducible automorphic cuspidal representation of
\GL_{d}({\mathbb{A}})
. Then the restricted tensor product

\displaystyle\Pi\coloneqq\sideset{}{{}^{\prime}}{\bigotimes}_{x}Q\left(\{\Pi_{0,x}[\textstyle\frac{t-1}{2}]\},\dotsc,\{\Pi_{0,x}[\frac{1-t}{2}]\}\right),

where
x
ranges over all places of
\mathbf{F}
, is an irreducible discrete automorphic representation of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
.

8.12.

We apply Lemma
8.11
to embed Speh modules into global representations.

Lemma
.

Let
\pi
be a Speh module, as a representation of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
. Then there exists an irreducible discrete automorphic representation
\Pi
of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
whose

(i)

component at
o
is isomorphic to
\pi
,

(ii)

components at
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
are Speh modules, as representations of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x_{1}})
,
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x_{2}})
, and
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty})
, respectively.

Proof.

Write
\pi
as
\pi=\Sp_{t}(\pi_{0})
, where
t
is some positive divisor of
n
, and
\pi_{0}
is an irreducible cuspidal representation of
\GL_{n/t}(\mathbf{F}_{o})
with unitary central character. Now Lemma
8.10
provides an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation
\widetilde{\Pi}_{0}
of
\GL_{n/t}({\mathbb{A}})
whose component at
o
is isomorphic to
\pi_{0}
and whose components at
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
are irreducible cuspidal representations of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x_{1}})
,
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x_{2}})
, and
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty})
, respectively, with unitary central characters. Then restricted tensor product
\Pi
associated with
\Pi_{0}
as in Lemma
8.11
has components

\displaystyle\Pi_{o}=\Sp_{t}(\pi_{0})=\pi,\,\Pi_{x_{1}}=\Sp_{t}(\Pi_{0,x_{1}}),\,\Pi_{x_{2}}=\Sp_{t}(\Pi_{0,x_{2}}),\mbox{ and }\Pi_{\infty}=\Sp_{t}(\Pi_{0,\infty}),

as desired.
∎

8.13.

Return to the local notation of §
1
–§
4
. Thus
F
is a local field of positive characteristic,
{\mathcal{O}}
denotes its ring of integers,
\varpi
is a fixed uniformizer, and
\kappa
denotes
{\mathcal{O}}/\varpi
. We will finally complete our proof of condition (c) in Lemma
2.7
by explicitly embedding our local situation into the global situation of
{\mathscr{D}}
-elliptic sheaves.

Proposition
.

Let
\pi
be a Speh module or an irreducible essentially
L^{2}
representation of
\GL_{n}(F)
. Then there exists an
n
-dimensional
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\geq 0}
-virtual continuous representation
\rho(\pi)
of
W_{F}
satisfying the trace condition

\displaystyle\tr(f_{\tau,h}|\pi)=\tr\left(\tau|\rho(\pi)\right)\tr(h|\pi)

for all
\tau
in
W_{F}
with
v(\tau)>0
and
h
in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}))
.

Proof.

Our goal is to apply Theorem B. Let
C={\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{\kappa}
be our curve of interest, choose
o
in
8.6
to be a
\kappa
-point of
C
, and choose
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
in
8.6
to be distinct closed points in
C\smallsetminus o
. Note that
\mathbf{F}_{o}
is isomorphic to
F
.

Let us first consider the case when
\pi
has unitary central character, that is,
\pi
is either a Speh module or
L^{2}
. Then Lemma
8.9
or Lemma
8.12
, respectively, yields an irreducible discrete automorphic representation
\Pi
of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
satisfying the hypotheses of Theorem B such that
\Pi_{o}
is isomorphic to
\pi
. Proceeding to apply Theorem B to
\Pi
shows that we may take
\rho(\pi)=\left.R(\Pi)\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}
.

In the last remaining case when
\pi
is an arbitrary irreducible essentially
L^{2}
representation
\pi
of
\GL_{n}(F)
, note that
\pi
is isomorphic to an unramified twist of an
L^{2}
representation. Thus the above work, along with Proposition
3.5
, concludes the proof in this case.
∎

By using Proposition
3.11
, Proposition
8.13
, and Proposition
4.11
to verify that conditions (b)–(d) of Lemma
2.7
hold and using Proposition
4.6
to check the
n=1
base case, this concludes our proof by induction of Theorem A.

9.
The second inductive lemma: bijectivity of the correspondence

Retain the local notation of §
1
–§
4
. The purpose of this section is to prove Theorem C, i.e. that
\pi\mapsto\rec\pi
yields a bijection from isomorphism classes of irreducible cuspidal representations of
\GL_{n}(F)
to isomorphism classes of
n
-dimensional irreducible continuous representations of
W_{F}
. We start by collecting some facts on restricting representations of
W_{F}
to representations of
W_{E}
for cyclic extensions
E/F
. Next, we use these facts to motivate
automorphic base change
, an analogous operation that turns representations of
\GL_{n}(F)
into representations of
\GL_{n}(E)
. Automorphic base change yields section’s first main result: a lemma which allows us to prove Theorem C by inducting on
n
, provided that we verify certain conditions.

We then proceed to verify the conditions needed for this inductive lemma, thus completing the proof of Theorem C. The main ingredients are Theorem A, Theorem B, and our nearby cycles calculation from §
6
. More precisely, we apply results from §
6
to show that the preimage of any unramified representation under
\rec
remained unramified, and we use Theorem B to show that
\rec
is compatible with automorphic base change as well as twisting by characters.

9.1.

We begin by establishing some notation on field extensions. Let
E
be a finite extension of
F
inside
F^{\text{sep}}
. For any finite-dimensional semisimple continuous representation
\rho^{\prime}
of
W_{E}
and any map
\alpha
in
G_{F}
, write
\rho^{\prime\alpha}
for the finite-dimensional semisimple continuous representation of
W_{\alpha^{-1}(E)}
given by
\tau\mapsto\rho^{\prime}(\alpha\circ\tau\circ\alpha^{-1})
for all
\tau
in
W_{\alpha^{-1}(E)}
.

Now assume that
E/F
is a cyclic extension. Hence
\alpha^{-1}(E)=E
. Write
r
for the degree of
E/F
, and fix a generator
\sigma
of
\Gal(E/F)=W_{F}/W_{E}
, which identifies it with
{\mathbb{Z}}/r{\mathbb{Z}}
. Write
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
for the set of group homomorphisms
\Gal(E/F)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
, and interpret
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
as a set of characters of
W_{F}
. Note that
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
acts on the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible continuous finite-dimensional representations of
W_{F}
via twisting.

When
r
is prime, one can use Frobenius reciprocity and the Mackey formula to verify the following assertions:

(i)

Let
\rho^{\prime}
be an irreducible continuous finite-dimensional representation of
W_{E}
. Then
\rho^{\prime}
extends to a representation
\rho
of
W_{F}
if and only if
\rho^{\prime}=\rho^{\prime\sigma}
. Furthermore, if this is the case, then there are
r
isomorphism classes of such
\rho^{\prime}
, and any two differ by a twist of a character in
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
.

(ii)

Let
\rho
be an irreducible continuous finite-dimensional representation of
W_{F}
. Then
\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}}
is reducible if and only if the stabilizer of
\rho
in
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
is nontrivial (and hence all of
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
). Furthermore, if this is the case, then

\displaystyle\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}}=\rho^{\prime}\oplus\dotsb\oplus\rho^{\prime\sigma^{r-1}}

for some irreducible continuous finite-dimensional representation
\rho^{\prime}
of
W_{E}
satisfying
\rho^{\prime}\neq\rho^{\prime\sigma}
, and
\rho
is the unique finite-dimensional semisimple continuous representation of
W_{F}
with this property.

We remark that the same argument works in the global setting, for which we shall use entirely analogous notation.

9.2.

Next, we recall
automorphic base change
in the local setting. For any finite extension
E
of
F
inside
F^{\text{sep}}
, any irreducible smooth representation
\pi^{\prime}
of
\GL_{n}(E)
, and any map
\alpha
in
G_{F}
, write
\pi^{\prime\alpha}
for the irreducible smooth representation of
\GL_{n}(\alpha^{-1}(E))
given by
g\mapsto\pi^{\prime}(\alpha(g))
for all
g
in
\GL_{n}(\alpha^{-1}(E))
. Note that
\Theta_{\pi^{\prime\alpha}}=\Theta_{\pi^{\prime}}\circ\alpha
.

As in
9.1
, specialize to cyclic extensions
E
of
F
, and adopt the notation of
9.1
as well. Since
\Art
induces an isomorphism
F^{\times}/\Nm_{E/F}(E^{\times})\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}\Gal(E/F)
, we can view
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
as a set of characters of
\GL_{n}(F)
via precomposition with
\operatorname{det}\circ\Art
. Thus
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
acts on the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible smooth representations of
\GL_{n}(F)
by twisting.

First, let
\pi
be an irreducible cuspidal representation of
\GL_{n}(F)
. Write
u
for the cardinality of the stabilizer of
\pi
in
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
. Then
u
divides
n
, and there exists an irreducible cuspidal representation
\pi^{\prime}
of
\GL_{n/u}(E)
such that

\displaystyle Q\left(\{\pi^{\prime}\},\dotsc,\{\pi^{\prime\sigma^{u-1}}\}\right)

is an irreducible generic representation of
\GL_{n}(E)
and is isomorphic to the base change lift of
\pi
to
\GL_{n}(E)

[
27
, (II.4.12, prop.)]
. In particular, the description of genericity from
2.2
indicates that
\pi^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
\pi^{\prime\sigma}
if and only if
u=1
. Note that this is the automorphic analogue of
9.1
.

9.3.

We now turn to local automorphic base change for generic representations. For any irreducible cuspidal representation
\tau
of
\GL_{d}(F)
, write
\Delta(\tau,m)
for the segment
\{\tau,\dotsc,\tau[m-1]\}
as in
[
49
, 3.1]
. Recall from
2.2
that every irreducible generic representation
\pi
of
\GL_{n}(F)
is of the form

\displaystyle\nInd_{P(F)}^{\GL_{n}(F)}\left(Q(\Delta(\pi_{1},m_{1}))\otimes\dotsb\otimes Q(\Delta(\pi_{t},m_{t}))\right),

where
n=d_{1}m_{1}+\dotsb+d_{t}m_{t}
, the
\pi_{i}
are irreducible cuspidal representations of
\GL_{d_{i}}(F)
, and
P
is the standard parabolic subgroup of
\GL_{n}
with block sizes
(d_{1}m_{1},\dotsc,d_{t}m_{t})
. Furthermore, none of the
\Delta(\pi_{i},m_{i})
are linked.

At this point, suppose that
\pi
also has
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
-regular segments as in
[
27
, (II.3.4)]
. Write
u_{i}
for the divisor of
d_{i}
and
\pi^{\prime}_{i}
for the irreducible cuspidal representation of
\GL_{d_{i}/u_{i}}(E)
associated with
\pi_{i}
as in
9.2
. Then the base change lift
\pi_{E}
of
\pi
to
\GL_{n}(E)
is isomorphic to
[
27
, (II.4.4)]
,
[
27
, (II.4.12, cor.)]

\displaystyle Q\left(\Delta(\pi^{\prime}_{1},m_{1}),\dotsc,\Delta(\pi_{1}^{\prime\sigma^{u_{1}-1}},m_{1}),\dotsc,\Delta(\pi^{\prime}_{t},m_{t}),\dotsc,\Delta(\pi_{t}^{\prime\sigma^{u_{t}-1}},m_{t})\right).

Note that
\pi_{E}
is an irreducible generic representation of
\GL_{n}(E)
.

Next, let
\xi
be any irreducible generic representation of
\GL_{n}(F)
with
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
-regular segments such that the base change lift of
\xi
to
\GL_{n}(E)
is isomorphic to
\pi_{E}
. Then
\xi
must be isomorphic to

\displaystyle\nInd_{P(F)}^{\GL_{n}(F)}\left(Q(\Delta(\chi_{1}\cdot\pi_{1},m_{1}))\otimes\dotsb\otimes Q(\Delta(\chi_{t}\cdot\pi_{t},m_{t}))\right)

for some
\chi_{1},\dotsc,\chi_{t}
in
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)

[
27
, (II.4.13, lem.)]
. Finally, every
\sigma
-stable irreducible generic representation
\pi^{\prime}
of
\GL_{n}(E)
is the base change lift of some irreducible generic representation of
\GL_{n}(F)
with
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
-regular segments
[
27
, (II.1.4)]
.

9.4.

We will also need automorphic base change in the global setting, for which we briefly return to our global notation. Hence
\mathbf{F}
is a global function field, and
{\mathbb{A}}
denotes its ring of adeles. Let
\mathbf{E}
be a cyclic extension of
\mathbf{F}
, write
{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbf{E}}
for the ring of adeles of
\mathbf{E}
, and fix a generator
\sigma
of
\Gal(\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F})=W_{\mathbf{F}}/W_{\mathbf{E}}
. Then
\sigma
acts on the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible discrete automorphic representations of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbf{E}})
via precomposition.

Let
\Pi
be an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
. Then there exists a unique irreducible discrete automorphic representation
\Pi_{\mathbf{E}}
of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbf{E}})
such that, for all places
o
of
\mathbf{F}
and places
o^{\prime}
of
\mathbf{E}
lying above
o
, the base change lift of
\Pi_{o}
to
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{E}_{o^{\prime}})
is isomorphic to
\Pi_{\mathbf{E},o^{\prime}}

[
27
, (IV.1.3)]
. Next, let
\Xi
be any irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
such that
\Xi_{\mathbf{E}}
is isomorphic to
\Pi_{\mathbf{E}}
. Then
\Xi
must be isomorphic to
\Pi\otimes(X\circ\operatorname{det})
for some character
X\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}/(\mathbf{F}^{\times}\Nm_{\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F}}({\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbf{E}}^{\times}))\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}

[
2
, Theorem III.3.1]
.
10
10

10

The proof given here is stated for number fields, but it only uses the relationship between unramified local
L
-functions and Satake parameters, as well as the fact that
L(\Pi\times\Pi^{\prime},s)
has a pole at
s=1
if and only if
\Pi\cong\Pi^{\prime\vee}
. In particular, it carries over to arbitrary global fields.
Finally, every
\sigma
-stable irreducible cuspidal representation
\Pi^{\prime}
of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbf{E}})
is isomorphic to
\Pi_{\mathbf{E}}
for some irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation
\Pi
of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
.

9.5.

Return to the local notation of §
1
–§
4
. We first verify that
\rec
is compatible with automorphisms.

Lemma
.

For any
\alpha
in
G_{F}
and any irreducible smooth representation
\pi^{\prime}
of
\GL_{n}(E)
, we have an isomorphism

\displaystyle\rec(\pi^{\prime\alpha})=\rec(\pi^{\prime})^{\alpha}.

Proof.

We immediately see
\alpha
preserves the Tate twist
(\textstyle\frac{1-n}{2})
, so it suffices to show that
\rho(\pi^{\prime\alpha})=\rho(\pi^{\prime})^{\alpha}
. We will do so by verifying that
\rho(\pi^{\prime})^{\alpha}
satisfies the defining property of
\rho(\pi^{\prime\alpha})
as in Theorem A.(i). Write
{\mathcal{O}}_{E}
for the ring of integers of
E
. Let
\tau
be an element of
W_{\alpha^{-1}(E)}
satisfying
r\coloneqq v(\tau)>0
, and let
h
be a function in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\GL_{n}(\alpha^{-1}({\mathcal{O}}_{E})))
. Now
3.4
yields

\displaystyle\tr\left(f_{\tau,h}|\pi^{\prime\alpha}\right)=\tr\left((\phi_{\tau,h},\sigma)|\pi^{\prime\alpha}/E_{r}\right)=\int_{\GL_{n}(\alpha^{-1}(E))}\!\dif\delta^{\prime}\,\phi_{\tau,h}(\delta^{\prime})\Theta_{\pi^{\prime\alpha}}(\mathrm{N}\delta^{\prime}),

where
\phi_{\tau,h}
is our test function in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\GL_{n}(\alpha^{-1}(E)_{r}))
from §
1
. Fixing a uniformizer
\varpi_{E}
of
E
and expanding the definition of
\phi_{\tau,h}
, we see that this integral equals

\displaystyle\int_{\GL_{n}(\alpha^{-1}({\mathcal{O}}_{E}))\diag(\alpha^{-1}(\varpi_{E}),1,\dotsc,1)\GL_{n}(\alpha^{-1}({\mathcal{O}}_{E}))}\!\dif\delta^{\prime}\,\tr(\tau\times h|[R\psi_{\delta^{\prime}}])\Theta_{\pi^{\prime}}(\alpha(\mathrm{N}\delta^{\prime})).

Upon making the change of variables
\delta=\alpha(\delta^{\prime})
, our integral becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{E})\diag(\varpi_{E},1,\dotsc,1)\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{E})}\!\dif\delta\,\tr(\tau\times h|[R\psi_{\alpha^{-1}(\delta)}])\Theta_{\pi^{\prime}}(\mathrm{N}(\alpha(\delta^{\prime})))

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\int_{\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{E})\diag(\varpi_{E},1,\dotsc,1)\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{E})}\!\dif\delta\,\tr((\alpha\circ\tau\circ\alpha^{-1})\times h\circ\alpha^{-1}|[R\psi_{\delta}])\Theta_{\pi^{\prime}}(\mathrm{N}\delta)

\displaystyle=\,

\displaystyle\int_{\GL_{n}(E)}\!\dif\delta\,\phi_{\alpha\circ\tau\circ\alpha^{-1},h\circ\alpha^{-1}}(\delta)\Theta_{\pi^{\prime}}(\mathrm{N}\delta)=\tr\left((\phi_{\alpha\circ\tau\circ\alpha^{-1},h\circ\alpha^{-1}},\sigma)|\pi^{\prime}/E_{r}\right).

Applying
3.4
once more indicates that this equals
\tr\left(f_{\alpha\circ\tau\circ\alpha^{-1},h\circ\alpha^{-1}}|\pi^{\prime}\right)
. From here, Theorem A.(i) shows that

\displaystyle\tr\left(f_{\alpha\circ\tau\circ\alpha^{-1},h\circ\alpha^{-1}}|\pi^{\prime}\right)=\tr\big(\alpha^{-1}\circ\tau\circ\alpha|\rho(\pi^{\prime})\big)\tr\big(h\circ\alpha^{-1}|\pi^{\prime}\big)=\tr\left(\tau|\rho(\pi^{\prime})^{\alpha}\right)\tr\big(h\circ\alpha^{-1}|\pi^{\prime}\big).

Thus all that remains is to prove that
\tr\big(h\circ\alpha^{-1}|\pi^{\prime}\big)=\tr\big(h|\pi^{\prime\alpha}\big)
. Now
\tr\big(h|\pi^{\prime\alpha}\big)
is the trace of the operator

\displaystyle v\mapsto\int_{\GL_{n}(\alpha^{-1}(E))}\!\dif g^{\prime}\,h(g^{\prime})\pi^{\prime}(\alpha(g^{\prime}))v=\int_{\GL_{n}(E)}\!\dif g\,h(\alpha^{-1}(g))\pi^{\prime}(g)v,

where we have made the change of variables
g=\alpha(g^{\prime})
. This shows that
\tr(h|\pi^{\prime\alpha})=\tr(h\circ\alpha^{-1}|\pi^{\prime})
, concluding our proof of Lemma
9.5
.
∎

9.6.

Finally, we can introduce our inductive lemma.

Second Inductive Lemma
.

Assume that the following conditions hold for all irreducible smooth representations
\pi
of
\GL_{n}(F)
:

(a)

Theorem C is true for
n^{\prime}<n
,

(b)

if
n=1
, then
\rec\pi
is isomorphic to
\pi\circ\Art^{-1}
,

(c)

if
\pi
is isomorphic to a subquotient of the normalized parabolic induction of

\displaystyle\pi_{1}\otimes\dotsb\otimes\pi_{t},

where the
\pi_{i}
are irreducible smooth representations of
\GL_{n_{i}}(F)
for which
n_{1}+\dotsb+n_{t}=n
. Then

\displaystyle\rec(\pi)=\rec(\pi_{1})\oplus\dotsb\oplus\rec(\pi_{t}),

(d)

for all smooth characters
\chi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}F^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
, we have

\displaystyle\rec(\pi\otimes(\chi\circ\operatorname{det}))=\rec(\pi)\otimes\rec(\chi),

(e)

if
\pi
is generic, then for all cyclic extensions
E/F
of prime degree, we have

\displaystyle\rec(\pi_{E})=\left.\rec(\pi)\right|_{W_{E}},

where
\pi_{E}
denotes the base change lift of
\pi
to
\GL_{n}(E)
,

(f)

if
\rec(\pi)
is an unramified representation of
W_{F}
, then
\pi
is an unramified representation of
\GL_{n}(F)
.

Then Theorem C is true for
n
.

In the first part of this section, our goal is to prove the second inductive lemma. To this end, starting from this point, assume that conditions (a)–(f) hold. We have already proved condition (b) in Proposition
4.6
and condition (c) in Theorem A. After proving Lemma
9.6
, we shall prove conditions (d)–(f) in the remainder of this section, using results from §
6
and §
8
.

9.7.

We begin by immediately upgrading condition (e) to finite solvable extensions
E/F
.

Proposition
.

Let
\pi
be generic. Then, for all finite solvable extensions
E/F
, we have

\displaystyle\rec(\pi_{E})=\left.\rec(\pi)\right|_{W_{E}},

where
\pi_{E}
denotes the base change lift of
\pi
to
\GL_{n}(E)
.

Proof.

We induct on the degree of
E
over
F
. The result is immediate for
E=F
, and condition (e) ensures that it holds for prime
[E\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}F]
. In general, the solvability of
E/F
yields a Galois subextension
E\supset E^{\prime}\supseteq F
such that
E/E^{\prime}
is cyclic of prime degree and
E^{\prime}/F
is solvable. Writing
\pi_{E^{\prime}}
for the base change lift of
\pi
to
\GL_{n}(E^{\prime})
, we see that
\rec(\pi_{E^{\prime}})=\left.\rec(\pi)\right|_{W_{E^{\prime}}}
by the inductive hypothesis. Transitivity of base change indicates that
\pi_{E}
is also the base change lift of
\pi_{E^{\prime}}
to
\GL_{n}(E)
, so condition (e) gives us

\displaystyle\rec(\pi_{E})=\left.\rec(\pi_{E^{\prime}})\right|_{W_{E}}=\left.\rec(\pi)\right|_{W_{E}}.\qed

9.8.

We make the following observation. Let
\rho
be a finite-dimensional semisimple continuous representation of
W_{F}
. Then
\rho
is smooth, so
\left.\rho\right|_{I_{E}}
is trivial for some finite Galois extension
E
of
F
. As
F
is a nonarchimedean local field,
E/F
is solvable. Altogether,
\rho
becomes unramified after passing to a solvable extension.

In particular, by letting
\rho=\rec\pi
for any irreducible generic representation
\pi
of
\GL_{n}(F)
, Proposition
9.7
and condition (f) imply that the base change lift
\pi_{E}
of
\pi
to
\GL_{n}(E)
is unramified.

9.9.

We now check that
\rec
sends irreducible cuspidal representations to irreducible ones.

Proposition
.

Suppose that
\pi
is irreducible cuspidal. Then
\rec\pi
is an irreducible representation of
W_{F}
.

Proof.

This is immediate for
n=1
, so suppose that
n\geq 2
. Now
9.8
yields a finite solvable extension
E/F
for which the base change lift
\pi_{E}
of
\pi
to
\GL_{n}(E)
is unramified. As
n\geq 2
, we see that
\pi_{E}
cannot be cuspidal. Therefore, by replacing
E/F
with a subextension if necessary, we can find a Galois subextension
E\supset E^{\prime}\supseteq F
satisfying the following properties:

\bullet

E/E^{\prime}
is cyclic of prime degree
r
,

\bullet

E^{\prime}/F
is solvable,

\bullet

the base change lift
\pi_{E^{\prime}}
of
\pi
to
\GL_{n}(E^{\prime})
remains cuspidal.

Since
\pi_{E}
is also the base change lift of
\pi_{E^{\prime}}
to
\GL_{n}(E)
, we can apply
9.2
to obtain a positive integer
u
and an irreducible cuspidal representation
\pi^{\prime}
of
\GL_{n/u}(E)
such that
\pi_{E}
is isomorphic to

\displaystyle Q\left(\{\pi^{\prime}\},\dotsc,\{\pi^{\prime\sigma^{u-1}}\}\right),

where
\sigma
is a generator of
\Gal(E/E^{\prime})
. Thus condition (e) and condition (c) indicate that
\rec(\pi_{E^{\prime}})
restricts to

\displaystyle\left.\rec(\pi_{E^{\prime}})\right|_{W_{E}}=\rec(\pi_{E})=\rec(\pi^{\prime})\oplus\dotsb\oplus\rec(\pi^{\prime})^{\sigma^{r-1}}.

Now
u
is the cardinality of a subgroup of
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/E^{\prime})
, so
u
divides
r
. But
\pi_{E}
is not cuspidal, which forces
u=r
. Hence
\pi^{\prime}
is not isomorphic to
\pi^{\prime\sigma}
. From here, Theorem C for
\pi^{\prime}
and Lemma
9.5
show that
\rec(\pi^{\prime})
is not isomorphic to
\rec(\pi^{\prime\sigma})=\rec(\pi^{\prime})^{\sigma}
, so
9.1
.(ii) indicates that
\rec(\pi_{E^{\prime}})
is irreducible.

Finally, because Proposition
9.7
shows that
\rec(\pi_{E^{\prime}})=\left.\rec(\pi)\right|_{W_{E^{\prime}}}
, we see that
\rec(\pi)
is irreducible as well.
∎

9.10.

Next, we establish a few lemmas on the bijectivity of
\rec
in special cases.

Lemma
.

Let
\rho
be an
n
-dimensional irreducible continuous representation of
W_{F}
, and let
E/F
be a cyclic extension of prime degree. If
\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}}
is reducible, then there exists a unique irreducible cuspidal representation
\pi
of
\GL_{n}(E)
such that
\rec\pi=\rho
.

Proof.

Write
r
for the degree of
E/F
, and fix a generator
\sigma
of
\Gal(E/F)
. Then
9.1
.(ii) yields an irreducible continuous finite-dimensional representation
\rho^{\prime}
of
W_{E}
satisfying
\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}}=\rho^{\prime}\oplus\dots\oplus\rho^{\prime\sigma^{r-1}}
and
\rho^{\prime}\neq\rho^{\prime\sigma}
. Now Theorem C for
\rho^{\prime}
provides a unique irreducible cuspidal representation
\pi^{\prime}
of
\GL_{n/r}(E)
such that
\rec(\pi^{\prime})
is isomorphic to
\rho^{\prime}
.

Define
\widetilde{\pi}
to be the irreducible smooth representation

\displaystyle\widetilde{\pi}\coloneqq Q\left(\{\pi^{\prime}\},\dotsc,\{\pi^{\prime\sigma^{r-1}}\}\right)

of
\GL_{n}(E)
. We see that
\widetilde{\pi}
is generic, and van Dijk’s formula
[
37
, Theorem 5.9]
implies that
\widetilde{\pi}
is
\sigma
-stable. Therefore
9.3
shows that
\widetilde{\pi}
is the base change lift of some irreducible generic representation
\pi
of
\GL_{n}(F)
. Furthermore, because the segments
\{\pi^{\prime}\},\dotsc,\{\pi^{\prime\sigma^{r-1}}\}
in
\widetilde{\pi}
have length
1
, our description of possibilities for
\pi
in
9.3
indicates
\pi
is cuspidal.

Condition (e), condition (c), and Lemma
9.5
yield

\displaystyle\left.\rec(\pi)\right|_{W_{E}}=\rec(\widetilde{\pi})=\rec(\pi^{\prime})\oplus\dotsb\oplus\rec(\pi^{\prime\sigma^{r-1}})=\rho^{\prime}\oplus\dotsb\oplus\rho^{\prime\sigma^{r-1}}.

The uniqueness of
\rho
from
9.1
.(ii) therefore indicates that
\rec\pi=\rho
. This takes care of the existence of
\pi
.

We now tackle uniqueness. Suppose
\xi
is any irreducible cuspidal representation of
\GL_{n}(F)
with
\rec(\xi)=\rho
, and write
\xi_{E}
for the base change lift of
\xi
to
\GL_{n}(E)
. Condition (e) and condition (c) show that
\rec(\xi_{E})=\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}}
is reducible, so Proposition
9.9
implies that
\xi_{E}
is not cuspidal. Therefore our description of base change in
9.2
gives

\displaystyle\xi_{E}=Q\left(\{\xi^{\prime}\},\dotsc,\{\xi^{\prime\sigma^{r-1}}\}\right)

for some irreducible cuspidal representation
\xi^{\prime}
of
\GL_{n/r}(E)
. Applying condition (e), condition (c), and Lemma
9.5
once more yields

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}\oplus\dotsb\oplus\rho^{\prime\sigma^{r-1}}=\left.\rec(\pi)\right|_{W_{E}}=\rec(\xi_{E})=\rec(\xi^{\prime})\oplus\dotsb\oplus\rec(\xi^{\prime\sigma^{r-1}})=\rec(\xi^{\prime})\oplus\dotsb\oplus\rec(\xi^{\prime})^{\sigma^{r-1}}.

Theorem C for
\xi^{\prime}
tells us that
\rec(\xi^{\prime})
is irreducible. As
\rho^{\prime}
is irreducible too, we see that
\rho^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
\rec(\xi^{\prime})^{\sigma^{i}}
for some
0\leq i\leq r-1
. After replacing
\xi^{\prime}
with
\xi^{\prime\sigma^{i}}
, we get
\rho^{\prime}=\rec(\xi^{\prime})
. Similarly replacing
\pi^{\prime}
with
\pi^{\prime\sigma^{i}}
for some
0\leq i\leq r-1
yields
\rho^{\prime}=\rec(\pi^{\prime})
, and hence
\pi^{\prime}=\xi^{\prime}
via Theorem C for
\rho^{\prime}
. This in turn yields
\widetilde{\pi}=\xi_{E}
. Since
\widetilde{\pi}
is the base change lift of
\pi
to
\GL_{n}(E)
, we see from
9.3
that
\xi
must be isomorphic to
\chi\cdot\pi
for some
\chi
in
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
. But the stabilizer of
\pi
in
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
has cardinality
r
by
9.2
. Thus we finally obtain
\xi=\chi\cdot\pi=\pi
, as desired.
∎

9.11 Lemma
.

Let
\rho
be an
n
-dimensional irreducible continuous representation of
W_{F}
, and let
E/F
be a cyclic extension of prime degree. Suppose that there exists a unique irreducible cuspidal representation
\widetilde{\pi}
of
\GL_{n}(E)
such that
\rec\widetilde{\pi}=\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}}
. If
\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}}
is irreducible, then there also exists a unique irreducible cuspidal representation
\pi
of
\GL_{n}(F)
such that
\rec\pi=\rho
.

Proof.

Write
r
for the degree of
E/F
, and fix a generator
\sigma
of
\Gal(E/F)
. As
9.1
.(i) indicates that

\displaystyle(\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}})^{\sigma}=\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}},

our uniqueness hypothesis on
\widetilde{\pi}
and
9.5
yield
\widetilde{\pi}^{\sigma}=\widetilde{\pi}
. Thus
\widetilde{\pi}
is
\sigma
-stable, so
9.3
shows that it is the base change lift of some irreducible generic representation
\pi
of
\GL_{n}(F)
. Furthermore, because
\widetilde{\pi}
is cuspidal, our description of possibilities for
\pi
implies that
\pi
is also cuspidal. Consequently, Proposition
9.9
indicates that
\rec\pi
is irreducible. Now condition (e) yields

\displaystyle\left.\rec(\pi)\right|_{W_{E}}=\rec(\widetilde{\pi})=\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}},

so
9.1
.(i) shows that
\rec(\pi)
is isomorphic to
\chi\cdot\rho
for some
\chi
in
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
. After replacing
\pi
with
\chi^{-1}\cdot\pi
, condition (d) tells us that
\rec\pi=\rho
. This takes care of the existence of
\pi
.

As for uniqueness, suppose
\xi
is any irreducible cuspidal representation of
\GL_{n}(F)
satisfying
\rec(\xi)=\rho
. Writing
\xi_{E}
for the base change lift of
\xi
to
\GL_{n}(E)
, we see that condition (e) gives us

\displaystyle\rec(\xi_{E})=\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}}=\rec(\widetilde{\pi}).

Condition (c) and the irreducibility of
\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}}
imply that
\xi_{E}
is cuspidal, so our uniqueness hypothesis on
\widetilde{\pi}
indicates that
\xi_{E}=\widetilde{\pi}
. As
\xi_{E}
is the base change lift of
\xi
to
\GL_{n}(E)
, we see from
9.3
that
\xi
must be isomorphic to
\chi\cdot\pi
for some
\chi
in
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
. From here, condition (d) yields

\displaystyle\rho=\rec\xi=\rec(\chi\cdot\pi)=\chi\cdot\rec\pi=\chi\cdot\rho,

and
9.1
along with the irreducibility of
\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}}
imply that
\chi=1
. Hence
\xi=\pi
, as desired.
∎

We shall finally wrap up the proof of Lemma
9.6
itself, that is, prove Theorem C holds for
n
.

Proof of Lemma
9.6
.

Proposition
9.9
shows that
\pi\mapsto\rec\pi
indeed yields a map from isomorphism classes of irreducible cuspidal representations of
\GL_{n}(F)
to isomorphism classes of
n
-dimensional irreducible continuous representations of
W_{F}
.

Next, we proceed towards bijectivity. Let
\rho
be an
n
-dimensional irreducible continuous representation of
W_{F}
. Bijectivity is immediate for
n=1
, so suppose that
n\geq 2
. Now
9.8
yields a finite solvable extension
E/F
for which
\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}}
is unramified. As
n\geq 2
, this implies that
\left.\rho\right|_{W_{E}}
cannot be irreducible. Thus, by replacing
E/F
with a subextension if necessary, we obtain a tower of field extensions
E=E_{s}\supset\dotsb\supset E_{0}=F
such that

\bullet

E_{j+1}/E_{j}
is cyclic of prime degree for all
0\leq j\leq s-1
,

\bullet

\left.\rho\right|_{E_{s-1}}
is irreducible,

\bullet

\left.\rho\right|_{E_{s}}
is reducible.

Lemma
9.10
provides a unique irreducible cuspidal representation
\pi_{s-1}
of
\GL_{n}(E_{s-1})
satisfying
\rec\pi_{s-1}=\left.\rho\right|_{E_{s-1}}
. From here, repeated applications of Lemma
9.11
yield unique irreducible cuspidal representations
\pi_{j}
of
\GL_{n}(E_{j})
such that
\rec\pi_{j}=\left.\rho\right|_{E_{j}}
, and the
j=0
case is precisely the desired result.
∎

Now that we have Lemma
9.6
, we turn our attention towards verifying that conditions (b)–(f) hold. Recall that we already proved condition (b) in Proposition
4.6
and condition (c) in Theorem A.

9.12.

To prove condition (d) and condition (e), we use the following explicit description of
\rec\pi
for unramified
\pi
.

Lemma
.

Let
\pi
be isomorphic to
Q\left(\{\chi_{1}\},\dotsc,\{\chi_{n}\}\right)
, where the
\chi_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}F^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
are unramified characters sending
\varpi
to
z_{i}
in
{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
. Then
\rec\pi
is isomorphic to the
n
-dimensional unramified representation of
W_{F}
where geometric
q
-Frobenius acts via
\diag(z_{1},\dotsc,z_{n})
. In particular, for all irreducible unramified representations
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
of
\GL_{n}(F)
, we have
L(\pi\times\pi^{\prime},s)=L(\rec(\pi)\otimes\rec(\pi^{\prime}),s)
.

Proof.

Theorem A shows that
\rec\pi
is isomorphic to
\rec(\chi_{1})\oplus\dotsb\oplus\rec(\chi_{n})
, and Proposition
4.6
allows us to conclude
\rec\pi
has the desired form. Next, any irreducible unramified representation
\pi
of
\GL_{n}(F)
is isomorphic to
Q\left(\{\chi_{1}\},\dotsc,\{\chi_{n}\}\right)
for some unramified characters
\chi_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}F^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}

[
12
]
. Write
z_{i}
for
\chi_{i}(\varpi)
, and form the analogous
\chi^{\prime}
and
z_{i}^{\prime}
for
\pi^{\prime}
too. Then we have

\displaystyle L(\pi\times\pi^{\prime},s)=\prod_{i,j=1}^{n}L(\chi_{i}\times\chi^{\prime}_{j},s)=\prod_{i,j=1}^{n}(1-z_{i}z_{j}q^{-s})^{-1}=L(\rec(\pi)\otimes\rec(\pi^{\prime}),s).\qed

9.13.

From here, we shall prove condition (d) and condition (e) via embedding into the global situation. More precisely, we will use Theorem B along with the Chebotarev density theorem. Let us recall some global notation:
C
denotes a geometrically connected proper smooth curve over
\kappa
,
\mathbf{F}
denotes its field of rational functions,
{\mathbb{A}}
denotes its ring of adeles, and
W_{\mathbf{F}}
denotes the Weil group of
\mathbf{F}
. By abuse of notation, we write
\Art\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}/\mathbf{F}^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}W_{\mathbf{F}}^{\text{ab}}
for the global Artin isomorphism normalized by sending uniformizers to geometric Frobenii. Fix distinct places
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
of
\mathbf{F}
.

9.14 Proposition
.

Let
\pi
be an irreducible smooth representation of
\GL_{n}(F)
. For all smooth characters
\chi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}F^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
, we have

\displaystyle\rec(\pi\otimes(\chi\circ\operatorname{det}))=\rec(\pi)\otimes\rec(\chi).

Proof.

We begin with some reductions. Theorem A indicates that both sides are compatible with parabolic induction, so it suffices to prove this for cuspidal
\pi
. As
\rec(\pi)=\rho(\pi)(\frac{1-n}{2})
and
\rec(\chi)=\chi\circ\Art^{-1}
, this is equivalent to

\displaystyle\rho(\pi\otimes(\chi\circ\operatorname{det}))=\rho(\pi)\otimes(\chi\circ\Art^{-1}).

Finally, note that
\chi
is the product of a finite order character with
{\lvert\cdot\rvert}^{s}
for some complex number
s
. The
\chi={\lvert\cdot\rvert}^{s}
case follows immediately from Proposition
3.5
, so we need only consider the case when
\chi
has finite order.

Assume this is the case, and let
C={\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{\kappa}
be our curve of interest. Choose
o
to be a
\kappa
-point of
C
, and choose
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
in
8.6
to be distinct closed points in
C\smallsetminus o
. Note that
\mathbf{F}_{o}
is isomorphic to
F
. Since
\chi
has finite order, there exists a finite order smooth character
X\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}/\mathbf{F}^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
such that
\left.X\right|_{\mathbf{F}_{o}^{\times}}=\chi

[
3
, Theorem 5 of Chapter X]
. Furthermore, as
\pi
is cuspidal, Lemma
8.10
provides an irreducible cuspidal representation
\Pi
of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
whose component at
o
is isomorphic to
\pi
and whose components at
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
are irreducible cuspidal representations of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x_{1}})
,
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x_{2}})
, and
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty})
, respectively, with unitary central characters.

Because the smooth character
X\circ\Art^{-1}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W_{\mathbf{F}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
has finite image, it extends to a continuous character
\Xi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}G_{\mathbf{F}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\times}
, where we identify
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
with
{\mathbb{C}}

[
27
, (IV.2.2)]
. Note also that
\Pi\otimes(X\circ\operatorname{det})
is an irreducible cuspidal representation of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
. Therefore we may apply Theorem B to obtain the
n
-dimensional semisimple continuous representations

\displaystyle R_{1}\coloneqq R(\Pi\otimes(X\circ\operatorname{det}))\mbox{ and }R_{2}\coloneqq R(\Pi)\otimes\Xi

of
G_{\mathbf{F}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
. Write
T
for the set of places
x
of
\mathbf{F}
such that

\bullet

\left.X\right|_{\mathbf{F}_{x}^{\times}}
is unramified,

\bullet

\Pi_{x}
is unramified,

\bullet

x
does not lie in
\{x_{1},x_{2},\infty\}
,

and note that
T
is cofinite. For
x
in
T
, Lemma
9.12
indicates that
\left.R_{1}\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{x}}}
and
\left.R_{2}\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{x}}}
are isomorphic.

The Chebotarev density theorem implies that, as
x
varies over all places of
\mathbf{F}
not lying in
T
, the conjugacy classes in
G_{\mathbf{F}}
of arithmetic
q_{x}
-Frobenius elements at
x
are dense. Because
n
-dimensional continuous semisimple representations of
G_{\mathbf{F}}
are determined, up to isomorphism, by their characteristic polynomials (and said polynomials are continuous in
G_{\mathbf{F}}
), we see that
R_{1}=R_{2}
as representations of
G_{\mathbf{F}}
. Restricting both sides to
W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}
and applying Theorem B again yields the desired result.
∎

9.15 Proposition
.

Let
\pi
be an irreducible generic representation of
\GL_{n}(F)
. For all cyclic extensions
E/F
of prime degree, we have

\displaystyle\rec(\pi_{E})=\left.\rec(\pi)\right|_{W_{E}},

where
\pi_{E}
denotes the base change lift of
\pi
to
\GL_{n}(E)
.

Proof.

Applying Theorem A to the decomposition of
\pi_{E}
from
9.3
