shows that it suffices to consider cuspidal
\pi
. With this reduction in hand, first set
C={\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{\kappa}
as our curve of interest, and choose a
\kappa
-point
o
of
C
. We may identify
\mathbf{F}_{o}
with
F
. By writing
E=F[t]/f(t)
and approximating
f(t)
using a polynomial with entries in
\mathbf{F}
, Krasner’s lemma yields a separable extension
\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F}
such that
o
is inert in
\mathbf{E}
, and
\mathbf{E}_{o^{\prime}}/\mathbf{F}_{o}
can be identified with
E/F
, where
o^{\prime}
is the unique place of
\mathbf{E}
dividing
o
. By replacing
\mathbf{E}
with its Galois closure and replacing
\mathbf{F}
(and changing
C
accordingly) with the subfield corresponding to the decomposition group of
o^{\prime}
, we may assume that
\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F}
is Galois and hence cyclic as well.

Since
\mathbf{E}
is a global function field, it corresponds to a geometrically connected proper smooth curve
C^{\prime}
over a finite field
\kappa^{\prime}
. Thus we can apply the results of §
5
–§
8
to
\mathbf{E}
and
C^{\prime}
. By the Chebotarev density theorem, we may choose
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
in
8.6
for
\mathbf{F}
to be distinct closed points in
C\smallsetminus o
such that they split completely in
C^{\prime}
. Choose
x_{1}^{\prime}
,
x_{2}^{\prime}
, and
\infty^{\prime}
in
8.6
for
\mathbf{E}
lying above
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
, respectively. This allows us to identify
\mathbf{E}_{x_{1}^{\prime}}
with
\mathbf{F}_{x_{1}}
,
\mathbf{E}_{x_{2}^{\prime}}
with
\mathbf{F}_{x_{2}}
, and
\mathbf{E}_{\infty^{\prime}}
with
\mathbf{F}_{\infty}
.

Because
\pi
is cuspidal, Lemma
8.10
gives an irreducible cuspidal representation
\Pi
of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
whose component at
o
is isomorphic to
\pi
and whose components at
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
are irreducible cuspidal representations of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x_{1}})
,
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x_{2}})
, and
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{\infty})
, respectively, with unitary central characters. As
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
split in
\mathbf{E}
, the irreducible discrete automorphic representation
\Pi_{\mathbf{E}}
formed in
9.4
has components

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathbf{E},x_{1}^{\prime}}=\Pi_{x_{1}},\,\Pi_{\mathbf{E},x_{2}^{\prime}}=\Pi_{x_{2}},\mbox{ and }\Pi_{\mathbf{E},\infty^{\prime}}=\Pi_{\infty}.

In particular, these components are irreducible cuspidal with unitary central characters. Hence we can apply Theorem B to
\Pi_{\mathbf{E}}
to form the
n
-dimensional semisimple continuous representations

\displaystyle R_{1}\coloneqq R(\Pi_{\mathbf{E}})\mbox{ and }R_{2}\coloneqq\left.R(\Pi)\right|_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}

of
G_{\mathbf{E}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
.

Write
T
for the set of places
x
of
\mathbf{F}
such that
\Pi_{x}
is unramified and
x
does not lie in
\{x_{1},x_{2},\infty\}
, which is a cofinite set of places. For all
x
in
T
, Lemma
9.12
and the description of base change lifts in
9.2
and
9.4
indicate that
\left.R_{1}\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{x}}}
is isomorphic to
\left.R_{2}\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{x}}}
. From here, we conclude using the Chebotarev density theorem and Theorem B, as in the proof of Proposition
9.14
.
∎

9.16.

Finally, we prove condition (f) using our results from §
6
.

Proposition
.

Let
\pi
be an irreducible smooth representation of
\GL_{n}(F)
. If
\rec(\pi)
is an unramified representation of
W_{F}
, then
\pi
is an unramified representation of
\GL_{n}(F)
.

Proof.

Write
\pi_{1},\dotsc,\pi_{t}
for the cuspidal support of
\pi
, where the
\pi_{i}
are irreducible cuspidal representations of
\GL_{n_{i}}(F)
such that
n=n_{1}+\dotsb+n_{t}
. Now Theorem A.(ii) yields

\displaystyle\rec(\pi)=\rec(\pi_{1})\oplus\dotsb\oplus\rec(\pi_{t}).

If
\pi
is not unramified, then there exists some
i
such that either

(1)

n_{i}=1
and
\pi_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}F^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
is not an unramified character,

(2)

n_{i}\geq 2
.

In case (1), Proposition
4.6
shows that
\rec(\pi_{i})
and hence
\rec(\pi)
is not unramified, so we need only tackle case (2). By replacing
\pi
with
\pi_{i}
, it suffices to assume that
\pi
is cuspidal and
n\geq 2
. Finally, because
\rec(\pi)
is an unramified twist of
\rho(\pi)
, we need only show that
\rho(\pi)
is not unramified.

We begin by using Schur orthogonality to obtain a function
h
in
C^{\infty}_{c}(\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}))
that satisfies
\tr(h|\pi)=1
. Then we have

\displaystyle\tr\left(\sigma^{-r}|\rho(\pi)^{I_{F}}\right)=\tr\left(\mathbf{1}_{\sigma^{-r}I_{F}}|\rho(\pi)\right)\tr(h|\pi)=\int_{\sigma^{-r}I_{F}}\!\dif\tau\,\tr\left(\tau|\rho(\pi)\right)\tr(h|\pi),

where
\mathbf{1}_{\sigma^{-r}I_{F}}
is the indicator function on
\sigma^{-r}I_{F}
. Applying Theorem A.(i), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\sigma^{-r}I_{F}}\!\dif\tau\,\tr\left(f_{\tau,h}|\pi\right),

and
3.4
indicates that this integral becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\sigma^{-r}I_{F}}\!\dif\tau\,\tr\left((\phi_{\tau,h},\sigma)|\pi/F_{r}\right)=\int_{\sigma^{-r}I_{F}}\!\dif\tau\,\int_{\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{r})\diag(\varpi,1,\dotsc,1)\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{r})}\!\dif\delta\,\phi_{\tau,h}(\delta)\Theta_{\pi}^{\sigma}(\delta).

Recall the subset
B_{n}\subseteq\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{r})\diag(\varpi,1,\dotsc,1)\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}}_{r})
, which was defined in
1.7
using the decomposition
\delta=\delta^{\circ}\oplus\delta^{\text{\'{e}t}}
. As
\pi
is cuspidal, we can use Lemma
4.10
to convert our integral into

\displaystyle\int_{\sigma^{-r}I_{F}}\!\dif\tau\,\int_{B_{n}}\!\dif\delta\,\phi_{\tau,h}(\delta)\Theta_{\pi}^{\sigma}(\delta).

Now let
C={\mathbb{P}}^{1}_{\kappa}
be our curve of interest, and choose a
\kappa
-point
o
of
C
. Since we may identify
\mathbf{F}_{o}
with
F
, the results of §
6
apply to our situation. Namely, Fubini’s theorem and Corollary
6.16
allow us to rewrite this integral as

\displaystyle\int_{\sigma^{-r}I_{F}}\!\dif\tau\,\phi_{\tau,h}(\delta)\int_{B_{n}}\!\dif\delta\,\Theta_{\pi}^{\sigma}(\delta)={\mathfrak{C}}_{n}\int_{B_{n}}\!\dif\delta\,\Theta_{\pi}^{\sigma}(\delta),

where
{\mathfrak{C}}_{n}
is as in Corollary
6.16
. The definition of
\Theta_{\pi}^{\sigma}
turns the above expression into

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{C}}_{n}\int_{B_{n}}\!\dif\delta\,\Theta_{\pi}(\mathrm{N}\delta),

and from here Lemma
Lemma
and the local Jacquet–Langlands correspondence yield

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{C}}_{n}(-1)^{n-1}\int_{B_{r}}\!\dif b\,\Theta_{\JL(\pi)}(b)={\mathfrak{C}}_{n}(-1)^{n-1}\tr\left(\mathbf{1}_{B_{r}}|\JL(\pi)\right)={\mathfrak{C}}_{n}(-1)^{n-1}\tr\left(\mathbf{1}_{B_{r}}|\JL(\pi)^{{\mathcal{O}}_{B}^{\times}}\right),

where
B
is the central division algebra over
F
of Hasse invariant
\frac{1}{n}
, and
B_{r}
is the subset of valuation
r
elements in
B
. Because
\pi
has no
\GL_{n}({\mathcal{O}})
-invariants, its image under the local Jacquet–Langlands correspondence has no
{\mathcal{O}}_{B}^{\times}
-invariants. Hence the above trace vanishes.

Altogether, we have shown that
\tr\left(\sigma^{-r}|\rho(\pi)^{I_{F}}\right)
vanishes for any positive integer
r
. As semisimple representations are determined by their traces, we have
\rho(\pi)^{I_{F}}=0
. In particular,
\rho(\pi)
is not unramified.
∎

By using Theorem A, Proposition
9.14
, Proposition
9.15
, and Proposition
9.16
to verify that conditions (b)–(f) of Lemma
9.6
hold and using Proposition
4.6
to check the
n=1
base case, this concludes our proof by induction of Theorem C.

10.
Duals,
L
-functions, and
\epsilon
-factors

In this section, our goal is to prove Theorem D, i.e. that
\pi\mapsto\rec\pi
satisfies Henniart’s properties
[
22
, Theorem 1.2]
characterizing the local Langlands correspondence for
\GL_{n}
over
F
. We begin by collecting facts on inducing representations of
W_{\mathbf{E}}
to representations of
W_{\mathbf{F}}
for separable extensions
\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F}
. Similar statements hold for extensions
E/F
of local fields. We use these facts to motivate
automorphic induction
, an analogous operation that turns representations of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbf{E}})
(respectively
\GL_{n}(E)
) into representations of
\GL_{n[\mathbf{E}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}]}({\mathbb{A}})
(respectively
\GL_{n[E\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}F]}(F)
) for cyclic extensions. Combining this with Theorem B allows us to prove automorphic induction for some non-Galois extensions
\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F}
.

We use this
non-Galois automorphic induction
to show that
\rec
is compatible with central characters. More precisely, we apply Brauer induction to reduce to the case of induced representations, embed into the global setting, and then invoke our non-Galois automorphic induction. Afterwards, we use compatibility with central characters to prove that
\rec
preserves
L
-functions and
\epsilon
-factors, by twisting with highly ramified characters. Finally, compatibility with duals follows from the decomposition of
L
-functions of pairs in terms of
L
-functions of characters. This concludes our proof of the local Langlands correspondence for
\GL_{n}
over
F
.

10.1.

Let us recall some global notation:
C
denotes a geometrically connected proper smooth curve over
\kappa
,
\mathbf{F}
denotes its field of rational functions,
{\mathbb{A}}
denotes its ring of adeles, and
W_{\mathbf{F}}
denotes the Weil group of
\mathbf{F}
.

Let
\mathbf{E}
be a finite extension of
\mathbf{F}
inside
\mathbf{F}^{\text{sep}}
. Now write
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}
for the Galois closure of
\mathbf{E}
over
\mathbf{F}
, and let
R\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W_{\mathbf{E}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
be a smooth character. One can use Frobenius reciprocity and the Mackey formula to show that
\Ind_{W_{\mathbf{E}}}^{W_{\mathbf{F}}}R
is irreducible if and only if the stabilizer of
\left.R\right|_{W_{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}}}
in
\Gal(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}/\mathbf{F})
equals
\Gal(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}/\mathbf{E})
. The same argument works in the local setting: let
E
be a finite extension of
F
in
F^{\text{sep}}
, and let
\rho\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W_{E}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
be a smooth character. Writing
\widetilde{E}
for the Galois closure of
E
over
F
, we see that
\Ind_{W_{E}}^{W_{F}}\rho
is irreducible if and only if the stabilizer of
\left.\rho\right|_{W_{\widetilde{E}}}
in
\Gal(\widetilde{E}/F)
equals
\Gal(\widetilde{E}/E)
.

10.2.

We begin by recalling
automorphic induction
in the local case. Let
E
be a cyclic
F
-algebra, and write
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
for the set of group homomorphisms
\Gal(E/F)\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
. By reducing to the case when
E
is a field, one can show that
\Art
induces an isomorphism
F^{\times}/\Nm_{E/F}(E^{\times})\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}^{\sim}\Gal(E/F)
, so
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
acts on isomorphism classes of smooth representations of
\GL_{n[E\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}F]}(F)
as in
9.2
.

Let
\pi
be an irreducible tempered representation of
\GL_{n}(E)
. Then there exists a unique irreducible tempered representation
I_{E/F}(\pi)
of
\GL_{n[E\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}F]}(F)
that is fixed by
{\mathfrak{K}}(E/F)
and satisfies a certain character identity involving
\Theta_{\pi}

[
26
, Theorem 1.3]
. This representation satisfies
L(I_{E/F}(\pi),s)=L(\pi,s)

[
26
, Theorem 1.4.(a)]
. Furthermore,
I_{E/F}(\pi)
is cuspidal if and only if the stabilizer of
\pi
in
\Gal(E/F)
is trivial
[
26
, Proposition 5.5]
. Note that this is the automorphic analog of
10.1
.

10.3.

Next, we introduce automorphic induction in the global setting. Let
\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F}
be a finite cyclic extension, and write
{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbf{E}}
for the ring of adeles of
\mathbf{E}
. Then for any irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation
\Pi
of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbf{E}})
, there exists a unique irreducible automorphic representation
I_{\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F}}(\Pi)
of
\GL_{n[\mathbf{E}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}]}({\mathbb{A}})
such that, for cofinitely many places
o
of
\mathbf{F}
for which
\Pi
is unramified at every place
o^{\prime}
of
\mathbf{E}
above
o
, we have
L(I_{\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F}}(\Pi)_{o},s)=\prod_{o^{\prime}\mid o}L(\Pi_{o^{\prime}},s)

[
27
, (IV.1.8)]
. In addition, for any place
o
of
\mathbf{F}
where the irreducible smooth representation
\Pi_{o}
of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{E}\otimes_{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{F}_{o})
is tempered, we have
I_{\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F}}(\Pi)_{o}=I_{\mathbf{E}\otimes_{\mathbf{F}}\mathbf{F}_{o}/\mathbf{F}_{o}}(\Pi_{o})

[
27
, (IV.1.9)]
.

10.4.

We establish some terminology that reflects the connection between automorphic and Galois representations.

Definition
.

Let
R
be an
n
-dimensional semisimple continuous representation of
G_{\mathbf{F}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
, and let
\Pi
be an irreducible automorphic representation of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}})
. We say
R
and
\Pi
are
associated
if there exists a finite set
T
of places of
\mathbf{F}
such that, for all places
x
of
\mathbf{F}
not in
T
,

(a)

\left.R\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{x}}}
and
\Pi_{x}
are unramified,

(b)

\rec\Pi_{x}
is isomorphic to
\left.R\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{x}}}
, where we identify
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
with
{\mathbb{C}}
.

Note that either one of
R
or
\Pi
determines the other, by the strong multiplicity one theorem and the Chebotarev density theorem. Furthermore, when there exist three places
\{x_{1},x_{2},\infty\}
of
\mathbf{F}
where
\Pi
is irreducible
L^{2}
or a Speh module, Theorem B shows that
R
is isomorphic to
R(\Pi)(\frac{1-n}{2})
and that condition (b) is true for all
x
not in
\{x_{1},x_{2},\infty\}
, even if
\left.R\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{x}}}
or
\Pi_{x}
are ramified.

10.5.

We view automorphic induction within the framework of Definition
10.4
as follows. Let
\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F}
be a finite cyclic extension. Let
R^{\prime}
be an
n
-dimensional continuous semisimple continuous representation of
G_{\mathbf{E}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
, and let
\Pi^{\prime}
be an irreducible automorphic representation of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbf{E}})
. If
R^{\prime}
and
\Pi^{\prime}
are associated, then Lemma
9.12
,
10.3
, and
10.2
show that
\Ind_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}R^{\prime}
and
I_{\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F}}(\Pi^{\prime})
are associated.

Next, let
X\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}/\mathbf{F}^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
be a finite order smooth character. The smooth character
X\circ\Art^{-1}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W_{\mathbf{F}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
has finite image, so it extends uniquely to a continuous character
\Xi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}G_{\mathbf{F}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}

[
27
, (IV.2.2)]
. Proposition
4.6
and the local-global compatibility of the Artin map imply that
\Xi
and
X
are associated.

Finally, let
R
be an
n
-dimensional semisimple continuous representation of
G_{\mathbf{F}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
, and let
\Pi
be an irreducible automorphic representation of
\GL_{n}({\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbf{F}})
such that
R
and
\Pi
are associated. Since any irreducible unramified representation of
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{F}_{x})
is isomorphic to
Q\left(\{\chi_{1}\},\dotsc,\{\chi_{n}\}\right)
for some unramified characters
\chi_{i}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}^{\times}_{x}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}

[
12
]
, Lemma
9.16
shows that
\rec\omega_{\Pi,x}=\operatorname{det}\left.R\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{x}}}
for cofinitely many places
x
of
\mathbf{F}
. Then the Chebotarev density theorem, Proposition
4.6
, and the local-global compatibility of the Artin map indicate that
\operatorname{det}{R}
is the unique extension of
\omega_{\Pi}\circ\Art^{-1}
to a continuous character
G_{\mathbf{F}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\times}
, where we identify
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\times}
with
{\mathbb{C}}
.

10.6.

We apply Theorem B via
10.4
to prove automorphic induction in a non-Galois setting.

Proposition
.

Let
\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F}
be a finite separable extension, and let
X\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbf{E}}^{\times}/\mathbf{E}^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
be a finite order smooth character. Assume that

(a)

the Galois closure
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}
of
\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F}
is solvable,

(b)

there exist three places
\{x_{1},x_{2},\infty\}
of
\mathbf{F}
inert in
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}
such that, for all
x
in
\{x_{1},x_{2},\infty\}
, the stabilizer of
\left.(X\circ\Art^{-1})\right|_{W_{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{e}}}}
in
\Gal(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{e}}/\mathbf{F}_{x})
equals
\Gal(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{e}}/\mathbf{E}_{e})
, where
\widetilde{e}
(respectively
e
) is the unique place of
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}
(respectively
\mathbf{E}
) lying above
x
.

Then there exists an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation
I_{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathbf{F}}(X)
of
\GL_{[\mathbf{E}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}]}({\mathbb{A}})
associated with
\Ind_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}(\widehat{X})
, where
\Xi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}G_{\mathbf{E}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
is the character associated with
X
as in
10.5
. Furthermore, the components of
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)
at
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
are irreducible cuspidal representations with unitary central characters.

Proof.

We induct on the degree of
\mathbf{E}
over
\mathbf{F}
, where the result is immediate for
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{F}
. In general, the solvability of
\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{F}
yields a Galois subextension
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}\supseteq\mathbf{K}\supset\mathbf{F}
such that
\mathbf{K}/\mathbf{F}
is cyclic of prime degree. Write
k
for the place of
\mathbf{K}
above
x
, and write
k^{\prime}
for the place of
\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}
above
x
. Then the stabilizer of

\displaystyle\left.(X\circ\Nm_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{E}}\circ\Art^{-1})\right|_{W_{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{e}}}}=\left.\Big(\left.(X\circ\Art^{-1})\right|_{W_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}}\Big)\right|_{W_{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{e}}}}=\left.(X\circ\Art^{-1})\right|_{W_{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{e}}}}

in
\Gal(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{e}}/\mathbf{K}_{k})
equals
\Gal(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{e}}/\mathbf{E}_{e})\cap\Gal(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{e}}/\mathbf{K}_{k})=\Gal(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{e}}/\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}_{k^{\prime}})
. Also, note that the unique extension of
X\circ\Nm_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{E}}\circ\Art^{-1}
to a continuous character of
G_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}
is
\left.\Xi\right|_{G_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}}
. Finally, as
[\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{K}]<[\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}]
, we can apply the inductive hypothesis to obtain an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation
I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}(X\circ\Nm_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{E}})
of
\GL_{[\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{K}]}({\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbf{K}})
associated with
\Ind_{G_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}}^{G_{\mathbf{K}}}(\left.\Xi\right|_{G_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}})
. Furthermore,
I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}(X\circ\Nm_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{E}})
is cuspidal with unitary central character at
k
. We now casework:

(1)

Suppose that
\mathbf{E}
contains
\mathbf{K}
. Then
\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{E}
, and we define
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)
to be
I_{\mathbf{K}/\mathbf{F}}(I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X))
. Now
10.5
indicates that
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)
and
\Ind^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}_{G_{\mathbf{K}}}\Ind_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}^{G_{\mathbf{K}}}(\Xi)=\Ind_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}(\Xi)
are associated. Because
x
is inert in
\mathbf{K}
and
I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)
is cuspidal at
k
, we see from
10.3
that
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)_{x}=I_{\mathbf{K}_{k}/\mathbf{F}_{x}}(I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)_{k})
. Note that the stabilizer of
I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)_{k}
in
\Gal(\mathbf{K}_{k}/\mathbf{F}_{x})
equals the image of
\Gal(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{e}}/\mathbf{E}_{e})
. Since
\mathbf{E}_{e}
contains
\mathbf{K}_{k}
, this is trivial, and hence
10.2
shows that
I_{\mathbf{K}_{k}/\mathbf{F}_{x}}(I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)_{k})
is cuspidal.

(2)

Suppose that
\mathbf{E}
does not contain
\mathbf{K}
. Then
\mathbf{K}\cap\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{F}
and
[\mathbf{E}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}]=[\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{K}]
, so
\Ind^{G_{\mathbf{K}}}_{G_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}}(\left.\Xi\right|_{G_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}})
is isomorphic to
\left.\Ind^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}(\Xi)\right|_{G_{\mathbf{K}}}
. Next, fix a generator
\sigma
of
\Gal(\mathbf{K}/\mathbf{F})
. As
I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}(X\circ\Nm_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{E}})
and
\left.\Ind^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}(\Xi)\right|_{G_{\mathbf{K}}}
are associated, there exist cofinitely many places
v
of
\mathbf{K}
where both are unramified and

\displaystyle\rec(I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}(X\circ\Nm_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{E}})_{v}^{\sigma})=\rec(I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}(X\circ\Nm_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{E}})_{v})^{\sigma}=\Big(\left.\Ind^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}(\Xi)\right|_{W_{\mathbf{K}_{v}}}\Big)^{\sigma}=\left.\Ind^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}(\Xi)\right|_{W_{\mathbf{K}_{v}}},

by Lemma
9.5
and
9.1
.(i). Then Lemma
9.12
indicates that the local representations
I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}(X\circ\Nm_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{E}})_{v}^{\sigma}
and
I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}(X\circ\Nm_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{E}})_{v}
are isomorphic. Hence the strong multiplicity one theorem shows that the global representations
I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}(X\circ\Nm_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{E}})^{\sigma}
and
I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}(X\circ\Nm_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{E}})
are isomorphic, so
9.4
yields an irreducible discrete automorphic representation
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)
of
\GL_{[\mathbf{E}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}]}({\mathbb{A}})
such that
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)_{\mathbf{K}}
is isomorphic to
I^{\mathbf{K}}_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}}(X\circ\Nm_{\mathbf{K}\mathbf{E}/\mathbf{E}})
. Because
\Gal(\mathbf{K}_{k_{\infty}}/\mathbf{F}_{\infty})=\Gal(\mathbf{K}/\mathbf{F})
, where
k_{\infty}
is the place of
\mathbf{K}
above
\infty
, Proposition
9.15
and
9.1
.(i) show that we may choose
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)
such that
\rec I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)_{\infty}=\left.\Ind_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}(\Xi)\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{\infty}}}
.

Now the base change lift of
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)_{x}
to
\GL_{[\mathbf{E}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}]}(\mathbf{K}_{k})
is cuspidal with unitary central character, so
9.3
indicates
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)_{x}
is also cuspidal with unitary central character. Thus we may apply Theorem B to obtain a semisimple continuous representation
\Sigma
of
G_{\mathbf{F}}
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
associated with
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)
. Proposition
9.15
implies that
\left.\Sigma\right|_{G_{\mathbf{K}}}
is isomorphic to
\left.\Ind^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}(\Xi)\right|_{G_{\mathbf{K}}}
, so
9.1
.(i) shows that
\Sigma
is isomorphic to
\Ind^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}(\Xi)
tensored with a character
\Gal(\mathbf{K}/\mathbf{F})\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
. By restricting to
W_{\mathbf{F}_{\infty}}
, we see that this character is trivial. Altogether,
I_{\mathbf{E}}^{\mathbf{F}}(X)
is associated with
\Sigma=\Ind^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}(\Xi)
.

Finally, as
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)
is cuspidal at one place, we see that
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)
itself is cuspidal.
∎

10.7.

We now introduce some notation for
{\mathbb{Z}}
-virtual representations of
W_{F}
. Write
{\mathscr{G}}_{F}
for the Grothendieck group of finite-dimensional continuous representations of
W_{F}
. Then
{\mathscr{G}}_{F}
is free over
{\mathbb{Z}}
, with a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-basis given by isomorphism classes of finite-dimensional irreducible continuous representations
\rho
of
W_{F}
. As every such
\rho
is of the form
\sigma(s)
for some complex number
s
and continuous representation
\sigma
of
G_{F}
, Brauer induction shows that
{\mathscr{G}}_{F}
is
{\mathbb{Z}}
-spanned by elements of the form
\Ind_{W_{E}}^{W_{F}}\chi
, where
E
runs over finite separable extensions of
F
, and
\chi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W_{E}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
runs over smooth characters
[
13
, 4.10]
.

The assignments
\rho\mapsto\operatorname{det}\rho
and
\rho\mapsto\rho^{\vee}
for finite-dimensional continuous representations
\rho
of
W_{F}
are additive. Therefore they extend to homomorphisms

\displaystyle\operatorname{det}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathscr{G}}_{F}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\Hom_{\text{cts}}(W_{F},{\mathbb{C}}^{\times})\mbox{ and }(-)^{\vee}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathscr{G}}_{F}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{G}}_{F}.

Write
{\mathfrak{M}}
for the field of meromorphic functions on
{\mathbb{C}}
, and fix a nontrivial continuous homomorphism
\psi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}F\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
. We also define
{\mathbb{Z}}
-bilinear maps

\displaystyle L(-\otimes-,s)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathscr{G}}_{F}\times{\mathscr{G}}_{F}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{M}}^{\times}\mbox{ and }\epsilon(-\otimes-,\psi,s)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathscr{G}}_{F}\times{\mathscr{G}}_{F}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{M}}^{\times}

by extending via
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linearity from their values on pairs
(\rho,\rho^{\prime})
of isomorphism classes of finite-dimensional irreducible continuous representations of
W_{F}
.

10.8.

The following forms an automorphic analogue of
{\mathscr{G}}_{F}
. Write
{\mathscr{A}}_{F}
for the free
{\mathbb{Z}}
-basis with generators given by isomorphism classes of irreducible cuspidal representations
\pi
of
\GL_{n}(F)
, where
n
ranges over all positive integers. The assignment
\pi\mapsto\rec(\pi)
extends to a map
\rec\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathscr{A}}_{F}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{G}}_{F}
, and Theorem C implies that this is an isomorphism.

Write
\omega_{\pi}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}F^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
for the central character of
\pi
. We define
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear maps

\displaystyle\omega_{\pi}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathscr{A}}_{F}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}\Hom_{\text{cts}}(F^{\times},{\mathbb{C}}^{\times})\mbox{ and }(-)^{\vee}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathscr{A}}_{F}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathscr{A}}_{F},

\displaystyle L(-\times-,s)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathscr{A}}_{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathscr{A}}_{F}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{M}}^{\times}\mbox{ and }\epsilon(-\times-,\psi,s)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathscr{A}}_{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}{\mathscr{A}}_{F}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathfrak{M}}^{\times}.

by extending via
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linearity from their values on isomorphism classes of irreducible cuspidal representations of
\GL_{n}(F)
, or pairs thereof.

10.9.

We now prove Theorem D, by using Proposition
10.6
and embedding into the global situation.

Proposition
.

(i)

For any smooth character
\chi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}F^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
, we have
\rec(\chi)=\chi\circ\Art^{-1}
.

(ii)

For any irreducible cuspidal representation
\pi
of
\GL_{n}(F)
and smooth character
\chi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}F^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
, we have

\displaystyle\rec(\pi\otimes(\chi\circ\operatorname{det}))=\rec(\pi)\otimes\rec(\chi).

(iii)

For any irreducible cuspidal representation
\pi
of
\GL_{n}(F)
, we have

\displaystyle\rec(\omega_{\pi})=\operatorname{det}\circ\rec(\pi)\mbox{ and }\rec(\pi^{\vee})=\rec(\pi)^{\vee}.

(iv)

For any irreducible cuspidal representations
\pi
of
\GL_{n}(F)
and
\pi^{\prime}
of
\GL_{n^{\prime}}(F)
, we have

\displaystyle L(\pi\times\pi^{\prime},s)=L(\rec(\pi)\otimes\rec(\pi^{\prime}),s)\mbox{ and }\epsilon(\pi\times\pi^{\prime},\psi,s)=\epsilon(\rec(\pi)\otimes\rec(\pi^{\prime}),\psi,s).

Proof.

We have already proved (i) in Proposition
4.6
and (ii) in Proposition
9.14
. Now both sides of (iii) are
{\mathbb{Z}}
-linear in
\pi
, and both sides of (iv) are
{\mathbb{Z}}
-bilinear in
(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
. Therefore
10.7
and
10.8
show that (iii) and (iv) follow from considering the same equations for
\pi=\rec^{-1}(\Ind_{W_{E}}^{W_{F}}\rho)
and
\pi^{\prime}=\rec^{-1}(\Ind_{W_{E^{\prime}}}^{W_{F}}\rho^{\prime})
in
{\mathscr{A}}_{F}
, where
E
and
E^{\prime}
are separable extensions of
F
, and
\rho\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W_{E}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
and
\rho^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}W_{E^{\prime}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
are smooth characters.

Note that
\rho
is the product of a finite order character with a Tate twist
(s)
for some complex number
s
. The projection formula and (ii) indicate that twisting
\rho
by
(-s)
results in twisting
\pi
by
(-s)
. Now (ii) implies that both sides of (iii) and (iv) are compatible with Tate twists, so we need only consider the case when
\rho
has finite order. In the same way, we may assume
\rho^{\prime}
also has finite order.

With this reduction in hand, write
\widetilde{E}
for the Galois closure of
E
. The proof of Proposition
9.15
yields a Galois extension
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}/\mathbf{F}
of global function fields and a place
z
of
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}
such that
z
is inert over
\mathbf{F}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{z}/\mathbf{F}_{o}
can be identified with
\widetilde{E}/F
, where
o
is the place of
\mathbf{F}
below
z
. Note then that
\Gal(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}/\mathbf{F})
is identified with
\Gal(\widetilde{E}/F)
. Writing
\mathbf{E}
for the subfield of
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}
corresponding to
\Gal(\widetilde{E}/E)
, we see that this identifies
\mathbf{E}_{y}
with
E
, where
y
is the place of
\mathbf{E}
below
z
. We apply this to
E^{\prime}
to similarly obtain extensions
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}\supseteq\mathbf{E}^{\prime}\supseteq\mathbf{F}^{\prime}
with places
z^{\prime}
,
y^{\prime}
, and
o^{\prime}
.

Because
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}/\mathbf{F}
is inert at one place
o
, the Chebotarev density theorem provides three more places
\{x_{1},x_{2},\infty\}
of
\mathbf{F}
that are inert in
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}
. For all
x
in
\{x_{1},x_{2},\infty\}
, there exists a finite order smooth character
\chi_{x}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{E}_{e}^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
such that the stabilizer of
\chi_{x}\circ\Nm_{\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{e}}/\mathbf{E}_{e}}
in
\Gal(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{e}}/\mathbf{F}_{x})
equals
\Gal(\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}_{\widetilde{e}}/\mathbf{E}_{e})
, where
\widetilde{e}
(respectively
e
) is the unique place of
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}
(respectively
\mathbf{E}
) lying above
x

[
19
, Lemma 4.7]
. As
\rho\circ\Art
also has finite order, there exists a finite order smooth character
X\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathbb{A}}_{\mathbf{E}}^{\times}/\mathbf{E}^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
such that
\left.X\right|_{\mathbf{E}_{y}^{\times}}=\rho\circ\Art
and
\left.X\right|_{\mathbf{E}_{x}^{\times}}=\chi_{x}
for all
x
in
\{x_{1},x_{2},\infty\}

[
3
, Theorem 5 of Chapter X]
. The same discussion yields analogous places
\{x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}^{\prime},\infty^{\prime}\}
of
\mathbf{F}^{\prime}
and an analogous finite order smooth character
X^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}{\mathbb{A}}^{\times}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}/\mathbf{E}^{\prime\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
.

Since
F
is a nonarchimedean local field,
\widetilde{E}/F
and hence
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}/\mathbf{F}
are solvable. Therefore we may apply Proposition
10.6
to obtain an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)
of
\GL_{[\mathbf{E}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbf{F}]}({\mathbb{A}})
associated with
\Ind_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}(\Xi)
, where
\Xi\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}G_{\mathbf{E}}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
is the character associated with
X
as in
10.5
. Furthermore, the components of
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)
at
x_{1}
,
x_{2}
, and
\infty
are irreducible cuspidal representations, so
10.4
shows that

\displaystyle\rec(I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)_{v})=\left.\Ind_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}(\Xi)\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{v}}}

for all places
v
of
\mathbf{F}
not in
\{x_{1},x_{2},\infty\}
. Taking
v=o
yields

\displaystyle\rec(I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)_{o})=\left.\Ind_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}(\Xi)\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}=\Ind^{W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}_{W_{\mathbf{E}_{y}}}(\left.X\right|_{\mathbf{E}^{\times}_{y}}\circ\Art^{-1})=\Ind^{W_{F}}_{W_{E}}\rho,

so
\pi
is the component of
I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)
at
o
. We apply this to
\mathbf{E}^{\prime}/\mathbf{F}^{\prime}
and
X^{\prime}
to similarly obtain an irreducible cuspidal automorphic representation
I^{\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})
associated with
\Ind^{G_{\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}}_{G_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}}(\Xi^{\prime})
.

Now
10.5
implies that
\omega_{I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)}
equals
\big(\operatorname{det}\Ind_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}(\Xi)\big)\circ\Art
, so restricting to
o
tells us that

\displaystyle\omega_{\pi}=\omega_{I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)_{o}}=\operatorname{det}\left.\Ind^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}(\Xi)\right|_{W_{\mathbf{F}_{o}}}=\operatorname{det}\Ind^{W_{F}}_{W_{E}}\rho=\operatorname{det}\rec\pi.

This completes the first part of (iii). Additionally, because
\big(\operatorname{det}\Ind_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}(\Xi)\big)\circ\Art
has finite image,
\omega_{I^{\mathbf{F}}_{\mathbf{E}}(X)}
does as well. And
10.1
shows that the restriction of
\Ind_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}(\Xi)
to
W_{\mathbf{F}_{\infty}}
is irreducible, so
\Ind_{G_{\mathbf{E}}}^{G_{\mathbf{F}}}(\Xi)
itself is irreducible. The same discussion holds for
I^{\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})
and
\Ind^{G_{\mathbf{F}^{\prime}}}_{G_{\mathbf{E}^{\prime}}}(\Xi^{\prime})
, so we can apply
[
25
, Theorem 2.4]
to conclude that (iv) holds.

Finally, return to the situation where
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
are irreducible cuspidal representations. Then

\displaystyle L(\pi\times\pi^{\prime},s)=\prod_{\chi}L(\chi,s),

where
\chi
ranges over unramified characters
F^{\times}\displaystyle\mathop{\longrightarrow}{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}
such that
\pi^{\prime\vee}\otimes(\chi\circ\operatorname{det})
is isomorphic to
\pi
. Since
L(\chi,s)
has a pole at
s=1
if and only if
\chi
is trivial, we see that
L(\pi\times\pi^{\prime},s)
has a pole at
s=1
if and only if
\pi^{\prime\vee}=\pi
. We have an analogous decomposition of
L(\rec(\pi)\otimes\rec(\pi^{\prime}),s)
, and the same argument indicates that
L(\rec(\pi)\otimes\rec(\pi^{\prime}),s)
has a pole at
s=1
if and only if
\rec(\pi^{\prime})^{\vee}=\rec(\pi)
. Applying (iv) completes the second part of (iii).
∎
